# 2.2.4 Model

- [SQL-PaLM: Improved Large Language Model Adaptation for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.00739): Combines few-shot Text-to-SQL prompting, instruction tuning with broader synthetic and database-content coverage, and execution-feedback test-time selection on Spider and BIRD.
- [Framework for evaluating code generation ability of large language models](https://doi.org/10.4218/etrij.2023-0357): Defines an automated LeetCode evaluation workflow plus a pass-rate granularity metric for comparing generated programs across prompt detail, problem date, and difficulty.
- [Federated Learning for Software Engineering: A Case Study of Code Clone Detection and Defect Prediction](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2023.3347898): Studies federated training for software-engineering predictors on code clone detection and defect prediction, where project data remain decentralized instead of pooled.
- [Enhancing Sentiment Analysis with Logistic Regression and NLP: A Case Study of 2023 Amazon Software Reviews](https://doi.org/10.1109/isncc62547.2024.10759007): Applies logistic regression and NLP preprocessing to sentiment classification over Amazon software reviews rather than contributing a code-generation model.
- [Text2Avatar: Text to 3d Human Avatar Generation with Codebook-Driven Body Controllable Attribute](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00711): Generates controllable 3D human avatars from text through a discrete codebook and pseudo avatar data from a pretrained 3D avatar generator.
- [Astraios: Parameter-Efficient Instruction Tuning Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.00788): Releases 28 OctoCoder variants tuned with seven full or parameter-efficient methods, comparing scale, LoRA trade-offs, robustness, security, and validation loss on code tasks.
- [Learning in the Wild: Towards Leveraging Unlabeled Data for Effectively Tuning Pre-Trained Code Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01060): Uses HINT, a hybrid pseudo-label selection and noise-tolerant training method, to tune pretrained code models with large unlabeled corpora for summarization, defect detection, and assertion generation.
- [De-Hallucinator: Iterative Grounding for LLM-Based Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.01701): Grounds code completion by retrieving project-specific API references from an initial prediction and re-prompting with increasingly relevant context to reduce nonexistent-API hallucinations.
- [AST-T5: Structure-Aware Pretraining for Code Generation and Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.03003): Pretrains encoder-decoder code models with AST-aware segmentation and span corruption, preserving program structure without changing the Transformer architecture.
- [Leveraging Print Debugging to Improve Code Generation in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05319): Prompts LLMs to insert print statements, inspect execution logs, and repair generated LeetCode solutions with an inference-time debugging loop.
- [CodePrompt: Improving Source Code-Related Classification with Knowledge Features through Prompt Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.05544): Uses prompt learning over CodeBERT-style representations, multi-layer knowledge features, and attention to classify source-code tasks without adding heavy task-specific layers.
- [Teaching Code LLMs to Use Autocompletion Tools in Repository-Level Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06391): Introduces ToolGen, which fine-tunes code LLMs to emit trigger tokens that call IDE autocompletion tools during repository-level generation.
- [Between Lines of Code: Unraveling the Distinct Patterns of Machine and Human Programmers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.06461): Introduces DetectCodeGPT, a machine-generated-code detector that perturbs code with spaces and newlines to exploit lexical, syntactic, and naturalness differences from human code.
- [Code Security Vulnerability Repair Using Reinforcement Learning with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07031): Trains vulnerability repair with reinforcement learning rewards that separately emphasize security fixes and preservation of syntactic and semantic functionality.
- [Your Instructions Are Not Always Helpful: Assessing the Efficacy of Instruction Fine-tuning for Software Vulnerability Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.07466): Tests whether instruction fine-tuning and natural-language prompts improve vulnerability detection and cross-language generalization on real-world vulnerable code.
- [KADEL: Knowledge-Aware Denoising Learning for Commit Message Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08376): Learns commit-message knowledge from good-practice commits and uses dynamic denoising to train on noisier commit data for message generation.
- [Code Generation with AlphaCodium: From Prompt Engineering to Flow Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.08500): Builds a test-based, multi-stage competitive-programming flow that iteratively reasons over problem specifications, candidate code, and generated tests.
- [BinaryAI: Binary Software Composition Analysis via Intelligent Binary Source Code Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.11161): Performs binary-to-source software composition analysis with two-phase matching that captures both syntactic and semantic signals for reused third-party libraries.
- [SpecLLM: Exploring Generation and Review of VLSI Design Specification with Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13266): Explores large-language-model generation and review of VLSI design specifications.
- [Investigating the Efficacy of Large Language Models for Code Clone Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.13802): Evaluates ChatGPT zero-shot prompts for mono-lingual and cross-lingual Type-4 clone detection on CodeNet-derived Java and Ruby pairs.
- [DeepSeek-Coder: When the Large Language Model Meets Programming - The Rise of Code Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14196): Trains code-specialized language models and analyzes how large language models support programming intelligence.
- [ZS4C: Zero-Shot Synthesis of Compilable Code for Incomplete Code Snippets Using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14279): Infers missing imports for incomplete Stack Overflow snippets and uses compiler feedback to repair import and syntax errors until the code compiles.
- [SparseCoder: Identifier-Aware Sparse Transformer for File- Level Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.14727): Introduces a sparse transformer with sliding-window, global, and identifier attention for long file-level code summarization, plus the FILE-CS dataset.
- [How Beginning Programmers and Code LLMs (Mis)read Each Other](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.15232): Reports a controlled study of 120 novice programmers showing where intent description, correctness judgment, and prompt repair break down with code LLMs.
- [OMPGPT: A Generative Pre-trained Transformer Model for OpenMP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16445): Builds a smaller domain-specific OpenMP code model for HPC parallel-program generation instead of relying on broad general-purpose code LLMs.
- [ReGAL: Refactoring Programs to Discover Generalizable Abstractions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16467): Learns reusable program abstractions by execution-preserving refactoring and iterative verification over a small set of existing programs.
- [IRCoCo: Immediate Rewards-Guided Deep Reinforcement Learning for Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.16637): Fine-tunes code-completion language models with deep reinforcement learning and immediate rewards to reduce exposure-bias error accumulation.
- [Commit Messages in the Age of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.17622): Benchmarks ChatGPT commit-message generation from code changes against earlier automatic message-generation methods.
- [Towards AI-Assisted Synthesis of Verified Dafny Methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.00247): Adapts pretrained models to synthesize Dafny methods and specifications that must satisfy the verifier rather than only compile.
- [DTS-SQL: Decomposed Text-to-SQL with Small Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01117): Decomposes Text-to-SQL into two fine-tuning stages so smaller open models can close part of the execution-accuracy gap to proprietary LLMs.
- [StepCoder: Improve Code Generation with Reinforcement Learning from Compiler Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01391): Uses compiler feedback in a stepwise reinforcement-learning framework to guide exploration for long code generation and avoid training on unusable snippets.
- [Code-Switched Language Identification is Harder Than You Think](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01505): Reformulates multilingual code-switched language identification as sentence-level multi-label tagging with lightweight models for corpus construction.
- [Code Representation Learning At Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01935): Scales code encoder pretraining with structure-aware masking followed by unsupervised contrastive learning using hard positive and hard negative examples.
- [Improving the Learning of Code Review Successive Tasks with Cross-Task Knowledge Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.02063): Improves code-review successive tasks through cross-task knowledge distillation.
- [Make Every Move Count: LLM-based High-Quality RTL Code Generation Using MCTS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03289): Adds Monte Carlo tree search to transformer decoding for RTL generation, optimizing compilability, functional correctness, and power, performance, and area.
- [BetterV: Controlled Verilog Generation with Discriminative Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03375): Improves controlled Verilog generation with discriminative guidance, adding an HDL-specific code-generation method.
- [Encoding Version History Context for Better Code Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03773): Encodes version-history context alongside source code representations to improve clone prediction and code classification with ASTNN and CodeBERT.
- [Automated Description Generation for Software Patches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.03805): Presents PATCHEXPLAINER, a machine-translation approach that encodes critical patch elements, history, syntax conventions, and clustered-description similarity for patch summaries.
- [Multi-line AI-Assisted Code Authoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.04141): Describes Meta CodeCompose's move from single-line to multi-line suggestions, including latency reduction, UI stability, and large-scale developer adoption metrics.
- [IRFuzzer: Specialized Fuzzing for LLVM Backend Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05256): Implements LLVM backend fuzzing with validity-preserving IR mutations, matcher-table coverage feedback, and target-specific guidance, reporting dozens of upstream bugs.
- [Machine Learning Augmented Branch and Bound for Mixed Integer Linear Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05501): Surveys learning-augmented branch-and-bound for MILP solvers, including branching, cutting planes, node selection, solver configuration, representations, and benchmarks.
- [Rocks Coding, Not Development: A Human-Centric, Experimental Evaluation of LLM-Supported SE Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05650): Runs a 109-participant controlled study showing ChatGPT helps simple coding more than broader software-development tasks.
- [Do Large Code Models Understand Programming Concepts? A Black-box Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.05980): Proposes CACP, a black-box counterfactual test suite for checking whether code LLMs understand programming concepts such as data flow and control flow.
- [How to Refactor this Code? An Exploratory Study on Developer-ChatGPT Refactoring Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06013): Mines 17,913 developer-ChatGPT conversations to classify refactoring intents, request patterns, and ChatGPT responses in refactoring work.
- [Unprecedented Code Change Automation: The Fusion of LLMs and Transformation by Example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07138): Uses LLM-generated semantic variants inside PyCraft to expand transformation-by-example inputs and automate recurring code-change patterns with static and dynamic checks.
- [Effort and Size Estimation in Software Projects with Large Language Model-based Intelligent Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07158): Uses LLM-based interfaces to expand UI-oriented user stories with data-source, interface, and algorithm details so development effort can be estimated more explicitly.
- [Efficient and Universal Watermarking for LLM-Generated Code Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07518): Proposes ACW, a training-free code watermark that applies semantic-preserving, idempotent transformations to generated code and detects them as implicit watermarks.
- [Sentinels of the Stream: Unleashing Large Language Models for Dynamic Packet Classification in Software Defined Networks - Position Paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07950): Outlines a planned Sentinel LLM for classifying software-defined-network packet contents by threat level, but the paper is a preliminary network-security position statement.
- [Grounding Data Science Code Generation with Input-Output Specifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08073): Fine-tunes code LLMs on synthetic data science programs using execution-derived input-output specifications as feedback for Arcade and DS-1000 tasks.
- [VerMCTS: Synthesizing Multi-Step Programs using a Verifier, a Large Language Model, and Tree Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08147): Guides Dafny and Coq program synthesis with modified Monte Carlo tree search, using verifier feedback on partial programs and measuring pass@T under a token budget.
- [Generative AI for Pull Request Descriptions: Adoption, Impact, and Developer Interventions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.08967): Analyzes 18,256 open-source pull requests using Copilot-generated descriptions, measuring adoption, review time, merge likelihood, and manual developer edits.
- [MPIrigen: MPI Code Generation through Domain-Specific Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09126): Fine-tunes MonoCoder on HPCorpusMPI with MPI-specific preprocessing to generate parallel C and C++ programs and evaluate function, location, and argument accuracy.
- [DolphCoder: Echo-Locating Code Large Language Models with Diverse and Multi-Objective Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09136): Instruction-tunes code LLMs on diverse reasoning paths plus a code-evaluation objective so the model learns both to generate and judge solutions.
- [Automated Unit Test Improvement using Large Language Models at Meta](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09171): Describes TestGen-LLM, an industrial tool that proposes unit-test improvements and filters them through build, reliability, and coverage checks before deployment.
- [Trained without My Consent: Detecting Code Inclusion in Language Models Trained on Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09299): Introduces TraWiC, a model-agnostic membership-inference classifier that uses syntactic and semantic code identifiers to detect whether code appeared in an LLM's training set.
- [Pinning “Reflection” on the Agenda: Investigating Reflection in Human–LLM Co-Creation for Creative Coding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09750): Studies reflection during LLM-assisted creative coding and finds decomposed subtask prompting induces more diagnostic and generative reflection.
- [Prompt Learning for Multi-Label Code Smell Detection: A Promising Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10398): Uses PromptSmell, mask-token prompt learning with customized answer verbalizers, to convert multi-label code smell detection into prompt-based classification.
- [Improving Demonstration Diversity by Human-Free Fusing for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10663): Introduces Fused, a human-free iterative synthesis method and diversity metric for building richer Text-to-SQL demonstration pools for in-context learning.
- [LLMDFA: Analyzing Dataflow in Code with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.10754): Decomposes dataflow analysis into LLM subtasks that synthesize helper code, call parsers and theorem provers, and summarize function-level dataflow facts.
- [Knowledge-to-SQL: Enhancing SQL Generation with Data Expert LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11517): Adds a tailored Data Expert LLM that reads database tables, supplies missing domain knowledge, and uses preference learning to improve text-to-SQL robustness.
- [Can ChatGPT Support Developers? An Empirical Evaluation of Large Language Models for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11702): Analyzes DevGPT shared conversations and finds LLM-generated code is mostly used for examples and concept demonstrations rather than production-ready code.
- [CodeArt: Better Code Models by Attention Regularization When Symbols Are Lacking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11842): Pretrains a BERT-like binary-code model with program-analysis-derived attention masks over dependence closures and token co-occurrences for stripped functions.
- [Enhancing Large Language Models for Text-to-Testcase Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.11910): Fine-tunes GPT-3.5 on curated requirement-to-testcase data with task-specific prompting, producing tests measured by syntax, requirement alignment, and coverage.
- [EvoR: Evolving Retrieval for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12317): Evolves both retrieval queries and multi-source knowledge bases so retrieval-augmented code generation adapts to newly updated libraries and APIs.
- [Scaling Laws Behind Code Understanding Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.12813): Tests code-understanding scaling laws by varying data, model size, and compute, then trains a larger code model after finding power-law error reduction.
- [Code Needs Comments: Enhancing Code LLMs with Comment Augmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13013): Augments pretraining corpora with generated comments and filters weak code-comment pairs to improve programming-language and natural-language alignment in code LLMs.
- [Do Machines and Humans Focus on Similar Code? Exploring Explainability of Large Language Models in Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14182): Compares programmer eye-tracking focus with SHAP token importance for code summarization and reports weak alignment between human and model attention.
- [REPOFUSE: Repository-Level Code Completion with Fused Dual Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14323): Fuses analogy context and locality context for repository-level code completion to improve suggestions while controlling inference latency.
- [RoboScript: Code Generation for Free-Form Manipulation Tasks across Real and Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14623): Provides a deployable robot-manipulation code-generation platform that connects LLM planning with perception, motion planning, control, and real-robot execution.
- [OpenCodeInterpreter: Integrating Code Generation with Execution and Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14658): Releases open code models trained with the 68K-interaction Code-Feedback dataset so generation, execution, and iterative refinement are integrated in one system.
- [Rethinking Software Engineering in the Foundation Model Era: A Curated Catalogue of Challenges in the Development of Trustworthy FMware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.15943): Catalogues ten industrial software-engineering challenges for FMware, including prompts, agents, orchestration, hallucination, productivity, cost, and risk.
- [CodeS: Towards Building Open-source Language Models for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16347): Builds open 1B-15B Text-to-SQL models with incremental SQL-centric pretraining, schema-linking treatment, and rapid domain-adaptation methods.
- [RoCoIns: Enhancing Robustness of Large Language Models through Code-Style Instructions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16431): Replaces ambiguous natural-language prompts with structured code-style instructions and adversarial-context demonstrations to improve LLM robustness under adversarial inputs.
- [CLAP: Learning Transferable Binary Code Representations with Natural Language Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.16928): Aligns assembly code with natural-language semantic explanations through contrastive language-assembly pretraining for transferable binary-code representations.
- [Constrained Decoding for Fill-in-the-Middle Code Language Models via Efficient Left and Right Quotienting of Context-Sensitive Grammars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.17988): Extends Earley parsing with left and right grammar quotients so fill-in-the-middle code generation can reject syntactically invalid Python completions early.
- [CEBin: A Cost-Effective Framework for Large-Scale Binary Code Similarity Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18818): Combines embedding-based candidate narrowing with comparison-based reranking to improve large-scale binary code similarity detection accuracy and cost.
- [StarCoder 2 and The Stack v2: The Next Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19173): Builds The Stack v2 from Software Heritage plus pull requests, notebooks, and documentation, then trains 3B, 7B, and 15B StarCoder2 code models on 3.3T to 4.3T tokens.
- [Compositional API Recommendation for Library-Oriented Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19431): Recommends library APIs compositionally to improve library-oriented code generation.
- [The Counterfeit Conundrum: Can Code Language Models Grasp the Nuances of Their Incorrect Generations?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.19475): Studies high-probability generated programs that pass weak checks yet remain wrong, showing code LMs often misjudge, mis-execute, and fail to repair these counterfeits.
- [Exploring Data-Efficient Adaptation of Large Language Models for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00046): Proposes DEED, an error-driven low-data adaptation method that uses model mistakes as training signals for scenario-specific code generation.
- [Semi-Instruct: Bridging Natural-Instruct and Self-Instruct for Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00338): Builds semi-instruction data by combining human-written natural instructions with self-generated instruction-code pairs to improve code LLM program synthesis.
- [Comparing Large Language Models and Human Programmers for Generating Programming Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.00894): Benchmarks seven LLMs against human programmers across prompts, languages, and task difficulty, showing GPT-4's strong but prompt-sensitive coding performance.
- [LLaMoCo: Instruction Tuning of Large Language Models for Optimization Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01131): Instruction-tunes LLMs for optimization-code generation using paired optimization problem prompts and effective solver code in a code-to-code format.
- [ContrastRepair: Enhancing Conversation-Based Automated Program Repair via Contrastive Test Case Pairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01971): Improves conversational program repair by feeding LLMs contrastive failing and passing test cases as targeted patch feedback.
- [Ivie: Lightweight Anchored Explanations of Just-Generated Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.02491): Adds instantly visible in-situ explanations beside generated code to speed developer inspection of programming-assistant outputs.
- [Design2Code: How Far Are We From Automating Front-End Engineering?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03163): Curates 484 real webpage screenshots and metrics to evaluate multimodal LLMs that convert visual designs into front-end code.
- [Fuzzing BusyBox: Leveraging LLM and Crash Reuse for Embedded Bug Unearthing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03897): Combines LLM-guided input generation with crash reuse to fuzz BusyBox versions used in embedded devices.
- [RepoHyper: Better Context Retrieval Is All You Need for Repository-Level Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.06095): Builds a repository-level semantic graph and context retrieval pipeline for code completion over files, classes, and hierarchy-aware project context.
- [Self-Adaptive, Requirements-Driven Autoscaling of Microservices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08798): Proposes MS-RA, a requirements-driven autoscaling controller for microservice replicas and resources rather than a code-generation model.
- [Bugs in large language models generated code: an empirical study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.08937): Empirically analyzes defects in LLM-generated programs, using the observed bug patterns as reliability evidence for code-generation models rather than proposing a new training recipe.
- [Unlocking the conversion of Web Screenshots into HTML Code with the WebSight Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09029): Introduces WebSight, a 2-million-pair synthetic HTML and screenshot dataset for training and evaluating screenshot-to-code VLMs.
- [UniCode: Learning a Unified Codebook for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.09072): Learns a unified visual and text codebook with language-driven iterative training and image-decompression pretraining for multimodal generation.
- [Repoformer: Selective Retrieval for Repository-Level Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.10059): Trains code LMs with self-supervised selective retrieval so they decide when repository context will help or hurt code completion.
- [Data is all you need: Finetuning LLMs for chip design via an automated design-data augmentation framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.11202): Generates Verilog and EDA-script training data automatically, then fine-tunes LLMs for hardware description code generation.
- [On the Effectiveness of Large Language Models for GitHub Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12446): Evaluates LLM generation of GitHub Actions workflows, including YAML validity, task semantics, and security issues specific to CI automation.
- [Enhancing Security of AI-Based Code Synthesis with GitHub Copilot via Cheap and Efficient Prompt-Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12671): Tests prompt-altering strategies for black-box code assistants such as GitHub Copilot to reduce security weaknesses in generated code.
- [Enhancing Code Generation Performance of Smaller Models by Distilling the Reasoning Ability of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.13271): Distills LLM-generated solution plans into smaller code models so they can use reasoning traces for programming problems without deploying a large teacher.
- [On the Generalizability of Deep Learning-based Code Completion Across Programming Language Versions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15149): Evaluates whether CodeT5-style code completion generalizes across nine Java versions as language syntax and APIs evolve.
- [Just another copy and paste? Comparing the security vulnerabilities of ChatGPT generated code and StackOverflow answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.15600): Compares security vulnerabilities in ChatGPT-generated snippets with StackOverflow answers to guide developer choices between code sources.
- [DeepFWI: Identifying Bug-Sensitive Warnings With Multi-Modal Code-Warning Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16032): Learns fine-grained bug-sensitive warning identification by combining source-code semantics with static-warning information instead of hand-crafted warning features.
- [Can Language Models Pretend Solvers? Logic Code Simulation with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16097): Evaluates whether LLMs can simulate logical solver execution by predicting outputs of logic programs rather than translating them to external solvers.
- [CoverUp: Effective High Coverage Test Generation for Python](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16218): Iteratively prompts LLMs with coverage analysis, code context, and feedback to generate Python regression tests with higher line and branch coverage.
- [Engineering Safety Requirements for Autonomous Driving with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16289): Uses an LLM prompt pipeline to refine, decompose, and review autonomous-driving safety requirements for redundancy and contradiction.
- [Reasoning Runtime Behavior of a Program with LLM: How Far are We?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16437): Evaluates LLMs on reasoning about program runtime behavior, exposing where execution-state prediction remains unreliable for code-understanding models.
- [CodeS: Natural Language to Code Repository via Multi-Layer Sketch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16443): Uses multi-layer sketches to bridge natural-language requirements and repository-level code generation across project structure, modules, and implementation details.
- [Investigating the readability of test code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16639): Reviews academic and grey literature on test-code readability and validates factors through a controlled experiment with practitioner context.
- [All Artificial, Less Intelligence: GenAI through the Lens of Formal Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16750): Applies formal verification to LLM-generated SystemVerilog hardware designs and labels common weakness exposure vulnerabilities across model-produced datasets.
- [Iterative Refinement of Project-Level Code Context for Precise Code Generation with Compiler Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.16792): Iteratively selects and refines project-level context using compiler feedback so code generation can repair missing dependencies and API mismatches.
- [CYCLE: Learning to Self-Refine the Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.18746): Trains code models to generate, critique, and revise their own solutions so self-refinement becomes a learned generation behavior rather than only a prompting trick.
- [PURPLE: Making a Large Language Model a Better SQL Writer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20014): Improves Text-to-SQL by teaching the model to use database evidence and query feedback when revising SQL.
- [Aurora-M: Open Source Continual Pre-training for Multilingual Language and Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00399): Continually pretrains an open multilingual language-and-code model so code ability is maintained alongside broader multilingual capability.
- [The Larger the Better? Improved LLM Code-Generation via Budget Reallocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.00725): Reallocates a fixed inference budget across sampling and selection to improve code-generation pass rates without training a larger model.
- [Stable Code Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01226): Documents Stable Code's code-specialized pretraining and instruction-tuning recipe for open software-development language models.
- [Automated User Story Generation with Test Case Specification Using Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01558): Generates user stories together with test-case specifications so requirements artifacts are paired with checkable acceptance behavior.
- [Understanding How CodeLLMs (Mis)Predict Types with Activation Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.01903): Uses activation steering to analyze and change how code LLMs predict static types, revealing internal signals behind type errors.
- [Towards Better Graph Neural Network-Based Fault Localization through Enhanced Code Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04496): Enhances graph neural fault-localization models with richer code representations so suspicious program locations are ranked more accurately.
- [Advancing Code Coverage: Incorporating Program Analysis with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.04966): Combines program-analysis signals with LLM-generated tests to target uncovered branches and improve code coverage.
- [The Fact Selection Problem in LLM-Based Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.05520): Studies which program facts should be fed to LLM repair systems so patches use relevant execution, test, and code-context evidence.
- [Behavior Trees Enable Structured Programming of Language Model Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07439): Represents language-model agent behavior as behavior trees, giving software developers a structured control formalism for composing and debugging agent programs.
- [A Multi-Expert Large Language Model Architecture for Verilog Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08029): Designs a multi-expert large language model architecture for Verilog code generation.
- [Revisiting Code Similarity Evaluation with Abstract Syntax Tree Edit Distance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08817): Reassesses code-similarity evaluation with AST edit distance to expose when embedding scores miss structural program differences.
- [Aligning the Objective of LLM-Based Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08877): Aligns LLM repair objectives with test-passing and patch-plausibility criteria so generated fixes optimize the actual repair target.
- [Test Code Generation for Telecom Software Systems Using Two-Stage Generative Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09249): Uses a two-stage generative model to produce telecom software tests from domain requirements and implementation context.
- [Generative transformations and patterns in LLM-native approaches for software verification and falsification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09384): Identifies recurring LLM-native transformation patterns for generating verification artifacts and falsifying candidate software behavior.
- [How Far Have We Gone in Binary Code Understanding Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.09836): Evaluates LLMs on binary-code understanding tasks to measure how much source-code knowledge transfers to compiled programs.
- [LLM-Powered Test Case Generation for Detecting Bugs in Plausible Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.10304): Generates tests for programs that look plausible but may be wrong, using LLMs to expose hidden bugs rather than only raising coverage.
- [A Deep Dive into Large Language Models for Automated Bug Localization and Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.11595): Analyzes how LLMs localize bugs and synthesize repairs, separating fault-location quality from patch-generation quality.
- [Exploring and Unleashing the Power of Large Language Models in Automated Code Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14646): Studies how large language models can be used for automated code translation.
- [NExT: Teaching Large Language Models to Reason about Code Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.14662): Trains LLMs on execution-trace reasoning so they can predict intermediate program states before generating or judging code.
- [Revisiting Unnaturalness for Automated Program Repair in the Era of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15236): Reexamines code unnaturalness as a signal for LLM-based program repair, testing whether language-model likelihood still helps identify fix locations and patches.
- [XFT: Unlocking the Power of Code Instruction Tuning by Simply Merging Upcycled Mixture-of-Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.15247): Merges upcycled mixture-of-experts models to strengthen code instruction tuning without training a code specialist from scratch.
- [AI Coders Are among Us: Rethinking Programming Language Grammar towards Efficient Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.16333): Revises programming-language grammar choices to make code generation more efficient for AI coding models.
- [How secure is AI-generated code: a large-scale comparison of large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18353): Compares major LLMs at scale on generated-code security, measuring vulnerability patterns across models and programming tasks.
- [VERT: Verified Equivalent Rust Transpilation with Large Language Models as Few-Shot Learners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18852): Uses large language models as few-shot learners for verified equivalent Rust transpilation.
- [Performance-Aligned LLMs for Generating Fast HPC Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.18864): Aligns code generation with performance feedback so LLM-produced HPC programs optimize speed as well as functional correctness.
- [Calibration of Large Language Models on Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19318): Measures and improves confidence calibration for LLM code summarization so summary likelihood better reflects correctness.
- [Exploring Multi-Lingual Bias of Large Code Models in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19368): Tests code LLMs across programming languages to identify generation bias toward high-resource languages and uneven correctness.
- [Assessing LLMs in Malicious Code Deobfuscation of Real-world Malware Campaigns](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.19715): Evaluates LLMs on deobfuscating malware from real campaigns, focusing on security-code understanding rather than benign coding tasks.
- [Constrained Decoding for Secure Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00218): Applies security constraints during decoding so code generation avoids unsafe APIs and vulnerability patterns before a candidate is emitted.
- [CodeHalu: Code Hallucinations in LLMs Driven by Execution-based Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00253): Uses execution-based verification to characterize code hallucinations such as nonexistent APIs, wrong dependencies, and semantically invalid snippets.
- [CC2Vec: Combining Typed Tokens with Contrastive Learning for Effective Code Clone Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00428): Combines typed token representations with contrastive learning to improve code-clone embeddings.
- [Prioritizing Software Requirements Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01564): Uses LLMs to rank software requirements by priority, turning stakeholder text into a requirement-ordering signal.
- [CodeFort: Robust Training for Code Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01567): Proposes robust training for code-generation models.
- [Class-Level Code Generation from Natural Language Using Iterative, Tool-Enhanced Reasoning over Repository](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01573): Generates class-level code from natural language by iteratively reasoning over repository context and tool feedback.
- [On the Limitations of Embedding Based Methods for Measuring Functional Correctness for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01580): Shows that embedding similarity is a weak proxy for functional correctness in code generation when behavior differs despite textual closeness.
- [Automated Control Logic Test Case Generation using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.01874): Uses LLMs to generate test cases for control-logic software from structured requirements and expected behavior.
- [Automatic Programming: Large Language Models and Beyond](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.02213): Frames automatic programming around LLM code synthesis while contrasting neural generation with search, verification, and programming-language techniques.
- [TOGLL: Correct and Strong Test Oracle Generation with LLMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03786): Generates test oracles with LLMs and filters them for both correctness and fault-detection strength.
- [Sketch Then Generate: Providing Incremental User Feedback and Guiding LLM Code Generation through Language-Oriented Code Sketches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.03998): Guides code generation with language-oriented sketches that let users give incremental structural feedback before full code is emitted.
- [Refining Joint Text and Source Code Embeddings for Retrieval Task with Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04126): Uses parameter-efficient fine-tuning to align text and source-code embeddings for retrieval tasks.
- [Granite Code Models: A Family of Open Foundation Models for Code Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.04324): Releases IBM's Granite Code foundation models, trained for code generation, explanation, fixing, and translation across model sizes.
- [Lessons from the Use of Natural Language Inference (NLI) in Requirements Engineering Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.05135): Analyzes where NLI-style models help requirements engineering tasks such as consistency checking, conflict detection, and traceability.
- [MCS-SQL: Leveraging Multiple Prompts and Multiple-Choice Selection For Text-to-SQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07467): Uses multiple prompts and multiple-choice selection to pick more reliable SQL candidates from LLM generations.
- [Prompt-Based Code Completion via Multi-Retrieval Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07530): Retrieves multiple kinds of code context and injects them into prompts for code completion.
- [MuMath-Code: Combining Tool-Use Large Language Models with Multi-perspective Data Augmentation for Mathematical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.07551): Combines code-oriented tool use with multi-perspective data augmentation to strengthen mathematical reasoning models.
- [Full Line Code Completion: Bringing AI to Desktop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.08704): Builds full-line code completion for desktop IDE use, balancing suggestion quality with latency and local deployment constraints.
- [SQL-to-Schema Enhances Schema Linking in Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09593): Generates SQL-like schema descriptions to improve schema linking before Text-to-SQL decoding.
- [Large Language Models for Automated Web-Form-Test Generation: An Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.09965): Empirically evaluates LLMs for generating automated tests that fill and validate web forms.
- [IntelliExplain: Enhancing Interactive Code Generation through Natural Language Explanations for Non-Professional Programmers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.10250): Adds natural-language explanations to interactive code generation so non-professional programmers can inspect and steer generated code.
- [Natural Is the Best: Model-Agnostic Code Simplification for Pre-trained Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11196): Simplifies code into more natural forms before feeding it to pretrained LLMs, improving downstream code understanding without model changes.
- [MHPP: Exploring the Capabilities and Limitations of Language Models Beyond Basic Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.11430): Extends code-model evaluation beyond basic generation to programming tasks that require deeper reasoning and environment understanding.
- [A Transformer-Based Approach for Smart Invocation of Automatic Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14753): Uses a transformer classifier to decide when an automatic code-completion suggestion should be invoked.
- [AutoCoder: Enhancing Code Large Language Model with AIEV-Instruct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14906): Fine-tunes code LLMs with AIEV-Instruct data to improve instruction following and code-generation reliability.
- [AnalogCoder: Analog Circuit Design via Training-Free Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14918): Uses training-free LLM code generation to produce analog-circuit design scripts and candidate circuit implementations.
- [EffiLearner: Enhancing Efficiency of Generated Code via Self-Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15189): Improves generated-code efficiency by having the model iteratively optimize its own solutions.
- [Before Generation, Align it! A Novel and Effective Strategy for Mitigating Hallucinations in Text-to-SQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15307): Aligns schema and question representations before SQL generation to reduce hallucinated columns, tables, and query structures.
- [Generating Code World Models with Large Language Models Guided by Monte Carlo Tree Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15383): Uses Monte Carlo tree search to guide LLM generation of code world models that predict program behavior.
- [OMNI-EPIC: Open-endedness via Models of human Notions of Interestingness with Environments Programmed in Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15568): Uses code-programmed environments and learned interestingness models to drive open-ended generation.
- [GECKO: Generative Language Model for English, Code and Korean](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15640): Trains a generative model covering English, code, and Korean so code capability coexists with bilingual natural-language ability.
- [Model Cascading for Code: A Cascaded Black-Box Multi-Model Framework for Cost-Efficient Code Completion with Self-Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15842): Cascades cheaper and stronger black-box models with self-testing so code completion spends expensive calls only when needed.
- [HYSYNTH: Context-Free LLM Approximation for Guiding Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15880): Approximates LLM guidance with context-free signals to steer program synthesis more efficiently.
- [CHESS: Contextual Harnessing for Efficient SQL Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16755): Builds Text-to-SQL synthesis around contextual schema evidence and candidate pruning for more efficient SQL generation.
- [ReflectionCoder: Learning from Reflection Sequence for Enhanced One-off Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.17057): Trains on reflection sequences so one-shot code generation can internalize critique-and-revision behavior.
- [SpecTra: Enhancing the Code Translation Ability of Language Models by Generating Multi-Modal Specifications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18574): Improves LLM code translation by generating multimodal specifications as auxiliary supervision.
- [Training LLMs to Better Self-Debug and Explain Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.18649): Fine-tunes LLMs to produce self-debugging traces and explanations that help locate and fix code errors.
- [Large Language Models for Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19032): Studies LLM-based code summarization across prompt, model, and output-quality factors.
- [AlchemistCoder: Harmonizing and Eliciting Code Capability by Hindsight Tuning on Multi-source Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19265): Uses hindsight tuning over multi-source code data to elicit and harmonize code capability in LLMs.
- [Qiskit Code Assistant: Training LLMs for generating Quantum Computing Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19495): Trains a code assistant for Qiskit so LLMs generate quantum-computing programs with library-specific knowledge.
- [Source Code Foundation Models are Transferable Binary Analysis Knowledge Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19581): Shows that source-code foundation models can transfer useful representations to binary-analysis tasks.
- [Dataflow-Guided Retrieval Augmentation for Repository-Level Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19782): Retrieves repository context along dataflow edges to improve code completion with relevant definitions and usages.
- [From Symbolic Tasks to Code Generation: Diversification Yields Better Task Performers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19787): Diversifies symbolic-task data and transfers it into code generation, improving task performance through broader program-like supervision.
- [Divide-and-Conquer Meets Consensus: Unleashing the Power of Functions in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20092): Decomposes code generation into function-level subproblems and uses consensus among candidates to assemble stronger solutions.
- [Unveiling the Impact of Coding Data Instruction Fine-Tuning on Large Language Models Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.20535): Measures how instruction fine-tuning on coding data changes broader LLM reasoning behavior.
- [Towards LLM-Powered Verilog RTL Assistant: Self-Verification and Self-Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00115): Builds a Verilog RTL assistant that verifies generated hardware code and corrects failures through feedback.
- [Hybrid Automated Program Repair by Combining Large Language Models and Program Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00992): Combines LLM patch generation with program-analysis constraints to improve automated program repair.
- [SemCoder: Training Code Language Models with Comprehensive Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.01006): Trains code language models with comprehensive semantic information to improve code understanding and generation.
- [Verified Code Transpilation with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03003): Uses LLMs for transpilation while checking semantic equivalence between source and translated code.
- [CATCODER: Repository-Level Code Generation with Relevant Code and Type Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03283): Conditions repository-level generation on retrieved code and type context so edits fit surrounding APIs.
- [Synthetic Programming Elicitation for Text-to-Code in Very Low-Resource Programming and Formal Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03636): Elicits synthetic programming examples to train text-to-code models for low-resource programming and formal languages.
- [VerilogReader: LLM-Aided Hardware Test Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04373): Uses LLMs to read Verilog designs and generate hardware tests that exercise intended RTL behavior.
- [Learning Task Decomposition to Assist Humans in Competitive Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04604): Learns decompositions of competitive-programming problems so humans receive structured subtasks before coding.
- [Morescient GAI for Software Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.04710): Proposes a more disciplined generative-AI approach for software engineering that emphasizes evidence, traceability, and task fit.
- [RAG-Enhanced Commit Message Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.05514): Retrieves relevant change context before generating commit messages, improving specificity over diff-only generation.
- [$\textbf{PLUM}$: Improving Code LMs with Execution-Guided On-Policy Preference Learning Driven By Synthetic Test Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06887): Fine-tunes code LMs with execution-guided on-policy preference learning, using synthetic tests to reward correct programs.
- [GraphCoder: Enhancing Repository-Level Code Completion via Code Context Graph-based Retrieval and Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07003): Builds a code-context graph for repository retrieval, then feeds graph-selected context to the language model for completion.
- [VersiCode: Towards Version-controllable Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07411): Conditions code generation on target library or language versions so outputs match version-specific APIs.
- [UICoder: Finetuning Large Language Models to Generate User Interface Code through Automated Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.07739): Fine-tunes LLMs for UI code generation with automated feedback from rendered interfaces.
- [FAIL: Analyzing Software Failures from the News Using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08221): Uses LLMs to extract and classify software-failure information from news reports for failure analysis.
- [FuzzAug: Data Augmentation by Coverage-guided Fuzzing for Neural Test Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08665): Augments neural test-generation training data with coverage-guided fuzzing examples.
- [Towards Understanding the Characteristics of Code Generation Errors Made by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.08731): Categorizes LLM code-generation errors to separate syntax, semantic, API, and reasoning failure modes.
- [STALL+: Boosting LLM-based Repository-level Code Completion with Static Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10018): Boosts repository-level code completion by combining LLMs with static analysis.
- [We Have a Package for You! A Comprehensive Analysis of Package Hallucinations by Code Generating LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10279): Analyzes hallucinated package names in generated code and the ecosystem risk created when nonexistent dependencies are suggested.
- [Unlock the Correlation between Supervised Fine-Tuning and Reinforcement Learning in Training Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.10305): Studies how supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning interact when training code LLMs, including whether RL can generalize without the SFT stage.
- [WaDec: Decompiling WebAssembly Using Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11346): Fine-tunes an LLM on wat-to-C snippets to decompile WebAssembly into readable source-like code, improving recompilability and output similarity over prior Wasm decompilers.
- [CodeGemma: Open Code Models Based on Gemma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11409): Releases Gemma-based open code models, including 7B pretrained and instruction-tuned variants and a 2B completion model for fast code infilling.
- [DocCGen: Document-based Controlled Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11925): Breaks structured DSL generation into document retrieval and code generation, using enterprise DSL documentation to control YAML and JSON-style outputs.
- [On the Impacts of Contexts on Repository-Level Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11927): Introduces RepoExec plus an instruction-tuned dependency-use dataset and Dependency Invocation Rate to study executability, tests, and cross-file context use.
- [DeepSeek-Coder-V2: Breaking the Barrier of Closed-Source Models in Code Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11931): Extends open-source code intelligence with a stronger coding language-model family.
- [A Problem-Oriented Perspective and Anchor Verification for Code Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11935): Rebuilds optimization pairs around shared programming problems and adds anchor verification to balance runtime speedups against correctness loss.
- [Should AI Optimize Your Code? A Comparative Study of Current Large Language Models Versus Classical Optimizing Compilers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12146): Compares LLMs with classical optimizing compilers on a benchmark of optimization patterns and analyzes where AI-driven code optimization falls short.
- [Towards Better Code Understanding in Decoder-Only Models with Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12326): Adapts decoder-only code generators for code search and clone detection with CL4D contrastive learning over semantically similar code representations.
- [Code-Optimise: Self-Generated Preference Data for Correctness and Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12502): Uses self-generated preference data that jointly labels correctness and runtime, training code models to preserve pass rates while producing faster and shorter solutions.
- [Prose-to-P4: Leveraging High Level Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.13679): Frames prose-to-networking-code generation as an LLM route from natural-language requirements to high-level languages that can later compile to P4 or NPL.
- [Investigating the Transferability of Code Repair for Low-Resource Programming Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.14867): Studies distilled iterative code repair across high- and low-resource languages, showing repair gains are language-dependent and weakly tied to reasoning ability.
- [Bug In the Code Stack: Can LLMs Find Bugs in Large Python Code Stacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15325): Introduces BICS, a code Needle-in-a-Haystack benchmark that tests whether LLMs can locate simple syntax bugs inside large Python code stacks.
- [On Giant's Shoulders: Effortless Weak to Strong by Dynamic Logits Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.15480): Transfers task knowledge from multiple small fine-tuned models to a larger model through dynamic logits fusion, avoiding additional training of the large model.
- [UniCoder: Scaling Code Large Language Model via Universal Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.16441): Introduces UniCode, a program-like intermediate reasoning representation, and instruction data that help code LLMs bridge natural-language reasoning and executable code.
- [An Empirical Study of Unit Test Generation with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18181): Evaluates open-source LLMs for Java unit-test generation across 17 projects and prompting settings, highlighting where prompt design changes test quality.
- [Hierarchical Context Pruning: Optimizing Real-World Code Completion with Repository-Level Pretrained Code LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18294): Proposes hierarchical context pruning for repository-level code completion, preserving dependency structure while removing unnecessary implementation details from prompts.
- [Towards View-based Development of Quantum Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.18363): Proposes a view-based quantum-software process around a Single Underlying Model and quantum IDE, making it an adjacent SE methodology rather than a code-model training paper.
- [LLM Critics Help Catch LLM Bugs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00215): Uses LLM critics to catch LLM bugs, adding a model-side verifier signal for software agents.
- [Beyond Functional Correctness: Investigating Coding Style Inconsistencies in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.00456): Evaluates whether LLM-generated code that passes tests still violates human coding-style conventions, separating style inconsistency from functional correctness.
- [Coding for Intelligence from the Perspective of Category](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01017): Uses category-theory framing to connect coding, probability modeling, and intelligence, deriving a general view of coding as representation for model learning.
- [TCSR-SQL: Towards Table Content-aware Text-to-SQL with Self-retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01183): Adds self-retrieval to text-to-SQL: the model extracts content keywords, fuzz-searches databases with seed SQL, and encodes confirmed column values for generation.
- [Esale: Enhancing Code-Summary Alignment Learning for Source Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.01646): Improves source-code summarization by explicitly learning alignment between code tokens and natural-language summaries.
- [INDICT: Code Generation with Internal Dialogues of Critiques for Both Security and Helpfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02518): Uses paired safety and helpfulness critics, backed by code snippets and tools, to guide code generation away from insecure yet superficially helpful answers.
- [Meta Large Language Model Compiler: Foundation Models of Compiler Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02524): Releases LLM Compiler models trained on LLVM IR and assembly, then instruction-tuned to understand compiler behavior and code optimization tasks.
- [Let the Code LLM Edit Itself When You Edit the Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03157): Introduces Positional Integrity Encoding to update a code LLM's KV cache after live code edits, avoiding full recomputation on insertion, deletion, and multi-place editing tasks.
- [An Empirical Study on Capability of Large Language Models in Understanding Code Semantics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03611): Introduces EMPICA, which perturbs code with semantics-preserving and semantics-changing transformations to test whether code LLMs truly understand program semantics.
- [AutoBench: Automatic Testbench Generation and Evaluation Using LLMs for HDL Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.03891): Builds AutoBench, an LLM-based HDL testbench generator with hybrid testbench structure and self-checking logic for simulation-based hardware verification.
- [An Adaptive Language-Agnostic Pruning Method for Greener Language Models for Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04147): Introduces ALPINE, an adaptive language-agnostic pruning layer that compresses code-model input sequences to reduce compute on defect prediction and clone detection.
- [Code Hallucination](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.04831): Taxonomizes LLM code hallucinations and introduces HallTrigger, a black-box prompting technique that uses dynamic model attributes to elicit hallucinated code.
- [Code Less, Align More: Efficient LLM Fine-tuning for Code Generation with Data Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05040): Shows that clustering and metric-based pruning can remove most synthetic code fine-tuning data while largely preserving, and sometimes improving, code-generation accuracy.
- [InverseCoder: Unleashing the Power of Instruction-Tuned Code LLMs with Inverse-Instruct](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.05700): Uses Inverse-Instruct to have a fine-tuned code LLM generate new instructions for existing code responses, augmenting instruction-tuning data without a closed teacher model.
- [Rectifier: Code Translation with Corrector via LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07472): Improves code translation with a corrector stage driven by large language models.
- [LLaVA-NeXT-Interleave: Tackling Multi-image, Video, and 3D in Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07895): Trains a multimodal model for interleaved images, video, and 3D understanding, so its software-development relevance is off-axis rather than code-specific.
- [Source Code Summarization in the Era of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.07959): Systematically studies LLM-based code summarization, comparing automated evaluation methods, prompting strategies, and fine-tuning choices against human judgments.
- [Foundation Model Engineering: Engineering Foundation Models Just as Engineering Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08176): Argues for foundation-model engineering, treating data and models as software artifacts that need declarative interfaces and automated management methods.
- [DeepCodeProbe: Towards Understanding What Models Trained on Code Learn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.08890): Uses DeepCodeProbe to inspect syntax and representation learning in models for clone detection, code summarization, and comment generation.
- [Benchmarking Language Model Creativity: A Case Study on Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09007): Defines DENIAL PROMPTING and NEOGAUGE to measure convergent and divergent creativity of LLM code solutions on Codeforces-style problems.
- [On Mitigating Code LLM Hallucinations with API Documentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.09726): Mitigates code-LLM hallucinations by grounding generation in API documentation.
- [CodeV: Empowering LLMs With HDL Generation Through Multilevel Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.10424): Uses multilevel summarization to improve large-model generation of hardware-description language code.
- [MaPPing Your Model: Assessing the Impact of Adversarial Attacks on LLM-based Programming Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11072): Introduces the MaPP attack, showing that small malicious additions to coding prompts can make programming assistants emit vulnerable but otherwise correct code.
- [Revisiting the Impact of Pursuing Modularity for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.11406): Defines a quantitative modularity metric for generated programs and tests whether modular decomposition actually improves LLM code-generation performance.
- [A Novel HDL Code Generator for Effectively Testing FPGA Logic Synthesis Compilers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12037): Proposes LegoHDL, a syntax-valid HDL generator that tests FPGA logic synthesis compilers with more diverse circuit programs than earlier generators.
- [An Empirical Study of Static Analysis Tools for Secure Code Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12241): Evaluates C and C++ static application security testing tools on real vulnerability-inducing code changes to measure their usefulness for secure code review.
- [Case2Code: Scalable Synthetic Data for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12504): Introduces Case2Code, a scalable synthetic-data task where models infer code from input-output behavior generated by executing programs.
- [AI-Assisted SQL Authoring at Industry Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13280): Describes Meta's SqlCompose system, including schema-aware SQL completion models, fill-in-the-middle generation, and fine-tuning on internal analytics data.
- [Scaling Granite Code Models to 128K Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.13739): Extends Granite code models to 128K-token context with RoPE scaling, repository-level file packing, long-context pretraining, and instruction tuning.
- [ECCO: Can We Improve Model-Generated Code Efficiency Without Sacrificing Functional Correctness?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.14044): Builds an efficiency benchmark for natural-language code generation and history-based code editing, then compares prompting, feedback refinement, and execution-conditioned fine-tuning.
- [Empowering Agile-Based Generative Software Development through Human-AI Teamwork](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.15568): Presents AgileGen, an agile human-AI development loop that iteratively aligns incomplete user requirements with generated code and acceptance criteria.
- [OriGen: Enhancing RTL Code Generation with Code-to-Code Augmentation and Self-Reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.16237): OriGen uses code-to-code augmentation and self-reflection to build open RTL datasets, repair syntax and functionality errors, and improve open-source LLMs for Verilog generation.
- [Large Language Model for Verilog Generation with Code-Structure-Guided Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18271): Introduces VeriSeek, a Verilog LLM improved with code-structure-guided reinforcement learning over limited high-quality RTL training data.
- [Classification-Based Automatic HDL Code Generation Using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18326): Mitigates HDL-code hallucination by first classifying circuit type, then decomposing generation into expert-like design subprocedures assisted by EDA tools.
- [Patched MOA: optimizing inference for diverse software development tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18521): Introduces Patched Mixture of Agents, a model-agnostic inference optimization method that combines candidate responses to improve software-development task performance.
- [Generative AI Augmented Induction-based Formal Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.18965): Applies generative AI to induction-based formal verification, aiming to increase hardware-verification throughput for correctness proofs.
- [Logic Distillation: Learning from Code Function by Function for Decision-making Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19405): Distills reasoning by having large models instantiate instructions into discrete functions, build a function base, and transfer function-by-function logic to smaller models.
- [RLCoder: Reinforcement Learning for Repository-Level Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19487): Trains a repository-context retriever with reinforcement learning, using target-code perplexity under retrieved context as the feedback signal for code completion.
- [Enhancing Code Translation in Language Models with Few-Shot Learning via Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.19619): Uses retrieval-augmented few-shot learning to improve code translation by language models.
- [When to Stop? Towards Efficient Code Generation in LLMs with Excess Token Prevention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.20042): Introduces CodeFast, which detects unnecessary excess tokens during code generation and stops decoding early to reduce latency and wasted computation.
- [Genetic Instruct: Scaling up Synthetic Generation of Coding Instructions for Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.21077): Scales coding-instruction synthesis with an Instructor LLM, Coder LLM, and Judge LLM evolutionary loop, producing over 7.5M instruction-code pairs for fine-tuning.
- [A new approach for encoding code and assisting code understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.00521): Proposes a diffusion-inspired code-understanding paradigm to address autoregressive LLM weaknesses in planning, working memory, backtracking, and complex code reasoning.
- [LibreLog: Accurate and Efficient Unsupervised Log Parsing Using Open-Source Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.01585): LibreLog performs unsupervised log parsing with open-source LLMs, clustering log templates to reduce labeling cost, commercial-model privacy risk, and large-volume parsing expense.
- [SceneMotifCoder: Example-Driven Visual Program Learning for Generating 3D Object Arrangements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02211): Learns reusable visual programs from 3D scene examples to synthesize object arrangements, making it adjacent program synthesis rather than code-LLM training.
- [SpecRover: Code Intent Extraction via LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02232): SpecRover studies low-cost iterative specification inference inside an LLM agent, combining code search, project-structure evidence, and reviewer feedback to vet program-improvement patches.
- [Context Conquers Parameters: Outperforming Proprietary Llm in Commit Message Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.02502): Shows that open-source LLMs can match proprietary OMG-style commit-message generation when given rich repository context, highlighting context over parameter scale for this developer task.
- [TestART: Improving LLM-based Unit Testing via Co-evolution of Automated Generation and Repair Iteration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03095): TestART co-evolves LLM-generated unit tests through automated generation and repair, using template-based fixes, coverage feedback, and positive prompt injection to improve pass rate and coverage.
- [LogUpdater: Automated Detection and Repair of Specific Defects in Logging Statements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03101): LogUpdater detects four logging-statement defect types with a synthetic-defect classifier, then uses type-aware prompts and past logging updates to recommend automated repairs.
- [Synthesizing Text-to-SQL Data from Weak and Strong LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03256): SENSE improves text-to-SQL by instruction-tuning open LLMs on synthetic data from strong models plus weak-model error signals, using preference learning to boost domain generalization.
- [Improving Retrieval-Augmented Code Comment Generation by Retrieving for Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03623): Trains the retriever in retrieval-augmented code comment generation from generator feedback, so retrieved exemplars are optimized for downstream comment generation rather than independent similarity.
- [Automated Code Fix Suggestions for Accessibility Issues in Mobile Apps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03827): Combines accessibility-issue localization with code-change recommendations to generate repairs for mobile app accessibility defects.
- [CodexGraph: Bridging Large Language Models and Code Repositories via Code Graph Databases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.03910): Represents repositories as code graph databases so LLMs can query cross-file symbols, dependencies, and relationships during codebase reasoning.
- [The Evolution of Information Seeking in Software Development: Understanding the Role and Impact of AI Assistants](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04032): Empirically studies how AI assistants change developers' information-seeking behavior, so it is adoption evidence rather than a model-side coding method.
- [Investigating adversarial attacks in software analytics via machine learning explainability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04124): Uses machine-learning explainability to study adversarial attacks on software-analytics models, assessing robustness across models, feature importance, and attack scenarios.
- [The Struggles of LLMs in Cross-Lingual Code Clone Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04430): Revisits cross-lingual code clone detection with five LLMs, eight prompts, and embedding-model baselines to quantify where LLMs struggle across programming languages.
- [XMainframe: A Large Language Model for Mainframe Modernization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04660): XMainframe builds a COBOL and mainframe-specialized LLM with a dedicated data pipeline and MainframeBench for multiple-choice, QA, and COBOL summarization evaluation.
- [Natural Language Outlines for Code: Literate Programming in the LLM Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.04820): Introduces natural-language outlines as a bidirectional code-assistance surface, letting LLMs summarize functions, synchronize outline edits with code, and support navigation, search, and generation.
- [Hotfixing Large Language Models for Code: How Far Can Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning Go?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.05727): Defines hotfixing for code LLMs and studies parameter-efficient updates that reduce buggy code generation after release without full retraining or broad performance loss.
- [Virtual Compiler Is All You Need For Assembly Code Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06385): ViC continues pretraining CodeLlama as a virtual compiler on Ubuntu-package compilations, then uses the model to synthesize assembly-search data across source languages.
- ["You still have to study" - On the Security of LLM generated code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07106): Evaluates four LLMs on Python and JavaScript security tasks using MITRE CWE, showing prompt quality strongly affects whether generated code contains weaknesses.
- [Vercation: Precise Vulnerable Open-Source Software Version Identification Based on Static Analysis and LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07321): VERCATION combines program slicing with an LLM to identify vulnerability-relevant code in C and C++ patches before matching vulnerable open-source software versions.
- [The Death of Schema Linking? Text-to-SQL in the Age of Well-Reasoned Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.07702): Revisits text-to-SQL schema linking and finds modern long-context LLMs often perform better by retaining full schemas while using augmentation, selection, and correction instead of hard filtering.
- [PatUntrack: Automated Generating Patch Examples for Issue Reports without Tracked Insecure Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.08619): Generates vulnerable-code and patch examples from issue reports when the insecure code is not explicitly tracked, supplying repair demonstrations for security developers.
- [An exploratory study on fine-tuning large language models for secure code generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09078): Fine-tunes code LLMs on 14,622 C/C++ files from vulnerability-fixing commits, comparing full fine-tuning, LoRA, and IA3 for reducing insecure generated code.
- [Selective Prompt Anchoring for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09121): Selective Prompt Anchoring counters attention dilution in code generation by steering code LLMs back to user intent, improving Pass@1 across six models and six benchmarks.
- [Galápagos: Automated N-Version Programming with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09536): Uses LLMs to generate diverse program variants, validates correctness and equivalence, and assembles N-version binaries whose variants differ statically and at runtime.
- [MergeRepair: An Exploratory Study on Merging Task-Specific Adapters in Code LLMs for Automated Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09568): Studies merging task-specific parameter-efficient adapters so a code LLM can support automated repair without fully retraining separate models.
- [Bridging the Language Gap: Enhancing Multilingual Prompt-Based Code Generation in LLMs via Zero-Shot Cross-Lingual Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09701): Uses zero-shot cross-lingual transfer to improve code generation from non-English prompts, targeting multilingual programming inputs.
- [Icing on the Cake: Automatic Code Summarization at Ericsson](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.09735): Evaluates Ericsson Java-method summarization, contrasting ASAP's static-analysis and retrieval-augmented prompting with lighter method-body-only LLM summaries.
- [Factorized-Dreamer: Training A High-Quality Video Generator with Limited and Low-Quality Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10119): Trains a video generator from limited and noisy data, so it is off-axis for software-development model coverage.
- [Does Co-Development with AI Assistants Lead to More Maintainable Code? A Registered Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10758): Registers a controlled study protocol for whether AI-assisted co-development changes code maintainability, defining generated-code, human-code, and post-hoc human-maintenance comparisons.
- [To Code, or Not To Code? Exploring Impact of Code in Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.10914): Runs pretraining ablations from 470M to 2.8B parameters to measure how adding code data changes code benchmarks, reasoning tasks, world knowledge, and LLM-as-judge win rates.
- [Multi-Turn Interactions for Text-to-SQL with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11062): Studies interactive Text-to-SQL generation where users and LLMs refine questions, schemas, and SQL across multiple turns.
- [What can Large Language Models Capture about Code Functional Equivalence?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11081): Probes what LLMs capture about code functional equivalence, testing whether their representations and judgments align with semantic equivalence beyond surface similarity.
- [Automated Prompt Engineering for Cost-Effective Code Generation Using Evolutionary Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11198): Uses evolutionary search to optimize prompts for code generation, reducing manual tuning and model-call cost while preserving solution quality.
- [RePair: Automated Program Repair with Process-based Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11296): RePair frames automated program repair as process-based feedback, using intermediate repair signals rather than only final correctness to guide LLM patch generation.
- [HITS: High-coverage LLM-based Unit Test Generation via Method Slicing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11324): HITS improves LLM-based unit-test generation through method slicing, narrowing test targets so generated tests achieve higher coverage with less irrelevant context.
- [Leveraging Large Language Models for Enhancing the Understandability of Generated Unit Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.11710): Studies LLM support for making generated unit tests more understandable, targeting readability and maintainability rather than only test correctness or coverage.
- [Enhancing Automated Program Repair with Solution Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12056): Adds solution-design guidance to automated program repair, prompting LLMs to reason about the intended fix before emitting patch code.
- [AutoTest: Evolutionary Code Solution Selection with Test Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12125): Selects among generated programs by pairing LLM-produced solutions with generated tests, executing them, and using genetic search to rank candidates from consensus signals.
- [Search-Based LLMs for Code Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12159): Search-Based LLMs treat code optimization as a search problem, exploring candidate programs to improve generated code beyond one-shot decoding.
- [SQL-GEN: Bridging the Dialect Gap for Text-to-SQL Via Synthetic Data And Model Merging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12733): SQL-GEN bridges text-to-SQL dialect gaps by combining synthetic data generation with model merging, targeting cross-dialect robustness for SQL-specialized models.
- [E-code: Mastering Efficient Code Generation through Pretrained Models and Expert Encoder Group](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.12948): Builds a language model for competitive code-efficiency optimization with pretrained components and an expert encoder group targeting performance flaws.
- [DOCE: Finding the Sweet Spot for Execution-Based Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13745): Compares execution-based decoding objectives for code generation, including candidate generation, reranking, minimum Bayes risk decoding, unit-test filtering, and multi-candidate self-debugging.
- [CodeGraph: Enhancing Graph Reasoning of LLMs with Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13863): CodeGraph uses code as a graph-reasoning medium for LLMs, testing whether executable or structured code representations improve graph reasoning performance.
- [Sifting through the Chaff: On Utilizing Execution Feedback for Ranking the Generated Code Candidates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.13976): Ranks generated code candidates with execution feedback, using compiler or test outcomes to filter self-consistency samples before final code selection.
