- [Fire-Flyer AI-HPC: A Cost-Effective Software-Hardware Co-Design for Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14158): Describes AI-HPC system co-design for deep-learning infrastructure, making it adjacent infrastructure rather than a code model.
- [Is Functional Correctness Enough to Evaluate Code Language Models? Exploring Diversity of Generated Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14504): Analyzes diversity among functionally correct code generations as an additional evaluation signal beyond pass rates.
- [A Joint Learning Model with Variational Interaction for Multilingual Program Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.14515): Introduces a variational-interaction model for multilingual program translation, learning shared and language-specific representations to improve translation across programming languages.
- [MTMamba++: Enhancing Multi-Task Dense Scene Understanding via Mamba-Based Decoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15101): Builds Mamba-based decoders for dense scene understanding, making it vision-model work and off-axis for code-model coverage.
- [An Empirical Study on Self-correcting Large Language Models for Data Science Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15658): CoT-SelfEvolve iteratively refines data-science code with self-correction guided by chain-of-thought feedback from real programming problems, improving DS-1000 performance.
- [MR-Adopt: Automatic Deduction of Input Transformation Function for Metamorphic Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.15815): MR-Adopt uses LLMs to infer reusable input-transformation functions from hard-coded metamorphic tests, turning one-off relations into test-adequacy amplifiers.
- [Using Large Language Models to Create AI Personas for Replication and Prediction of Media Effects: An Empirical Test of 133 Published Experimental Research Findings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16073): Uses LLM personas to simulate media-effects experiments, which is social-science simulation rather than software-development model work.
- [Automatic Library Migration Using Large Language Models: First Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16151): Studies ChatGPT for automatic library migration, comparing zero-shot, one-shot, and chain-of-thought prompts on migrating SQLAlchemy client code.
- [Examination of Code generated by Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.16601): Inspects generated-code quality and failure modes across LLM outputs, contributing empirical evidence on code-model reliability.
- [Fixing Function-Level Code Generation Errors for Foundation Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00676): Analyzes function-level code-generation errors across 14 LLMs on HumanEval to identify root causes that static tools and repair LLMs still miss.
- [Dependency-Aware Code Naturalness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.00747): Models code naturalness with dependency awareness.
- [Beyond ChatGPT: Enhancing Software Quality Assurance Tasks with Diverse LLMs and Validation Techniques](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01001): Compares GPT-3.5, GPT-4o, LLaMA, Gemma, and Mixtral on fault localization and vulnerability detection, showing diverse LLM predictions can complement each other.
- [FuzzCoder: Byte-level Fuzzing Test via Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.01944): FuzzCoder fine-tunes code LLMs as byte-level sequence-to-sequence mutators, learning from successful attack inputs to guide fuzzing toward vulnerability-triggering cases.
- [Arctic-SnowCoder: Demystifying High-Quality Data in Code Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.02326): Pretrains Arctic-SnowCoder-1.3B through standard code filtering, BERT-style quality annotation for high-quality code, and Llama-generated synthetic enhanced data.
- [ASTER: Natural and Multi-Language Unit Test Generation with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03093): ASTER combines static analysis with LLM prompting to generate natural, compilable, high-coverage unit tests across Java, Python, and mocked environments.
- [No Man is an Island: Towards Fully Automatic Programming by Code Search, Code Generation and Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03267): Combines code search, code generation, and program repair toward automatic programming, mirroring developer workflows that use external search and testing tools.
- [How Do Your Code LLMs Perform? Empowering Code Instruction Tuning with High-Quality Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.03810): Builds higher-quality code instruction-tuning data and analyzes how data selection affects downstream Code LLM performance.
- [Multi-Programming Language Ensemble for Code Generation in Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04114): MPLE treats code generation in multiple programming languages as weak experts and ensembles their outputs to reduce language-specific errors in LLM solutions.
- [GALLa: Graph Aligned Large Language Models for Improved Source Code Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.04183): GALLa injects code-graph structure into code LLMs through graph neural networks and cross-modal alignment during fine-tuning, without changing the base Transformer architecture.
- [Insights from Benchmarking Frontier Language Models on Web App Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05177): Benchmarks 16 frontier LLMs on WebApp1K and argues coding-model progress should focus on reducing mistake frequency and improving reliability, not only knowledge coverage.
- [SGC-VQGAN: Towards Complex Scene Representation via Semantic Guided Clustering Codebook](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06105): Learns a semantic-guided clustering codebook for complex scene representation, so it belongs to visual representation learning rather than software-development models.
- [Exploring the Integration of Large Language Models in Industrial Test Maintenance Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06416): Studies industrial test maintenance at Ericsson, identifying maintenance triggers, LLM-supported actions, deployment considerations, and a multi-agent demonstration.
- [HexaCoder: Secure Code Generation via Oracle-Guided Synthetic Training Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06446): Synthesizes vulnerable and fixed code pairs with an oracle-guided pipeline, then uses a two-step secure-code generation process to reduce LLM security flaws.
- [E2LLM: Encoder Elongated Large Language Models for Long-Context Understanding and Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.06679): Extends LLM context handling by elongating the encoder side for long-context understanding and reasoning; it is general model work, not code-specific.
- [RethinkMCTS: Refining Erroneous Thoughts in Monte Carlo Tree Search for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09584): Searches over reasoning thoughts before code generation with MCTS, then refines erroneous thoughts so later search iterations follow corrected reasoning paths.
- [SmartC2Rust: Iterative, Feedback-Driven C-to-Rust Translation via Large Language Models for Safety and Equivalence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10506): Segments legacy C, translates it to Rust, and iteratively repairs the output with compiler and equivalence feedback to reduce unsafe code while preserving behavior.
- [Towards No-Code Programming of Cobots: Experiments with Code Synthesis by Large Code Models for Conversational Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11041): Tests large code models for conversational cobot programming, translating no-code user intents into robot-control code.
- [Ideal-LLM: Integrating Dual Encoders and Language-Adapted LLM for Multilingual Speech-to-Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11214): Integrates dual encoders with a language-adapted LLM for multilingual speech-to-text, making it off-axis for software-development code models.
- [RTLRewriter: Methodologies for Large Models Aided RTL Code Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.11414): Studies large-model-aided RTL code optimization methods.
- [Promise and Peril of Collaborative Code Generation Models: Balancing Effectiveness and Memorization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12020): Studies collaborative code generation while measuring the trade-off between task effectiveness and memorization of training code.
- [Qwen2.5-Coder Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12186): Documents a code-specialized Qwen model family and large-scale code instruction tuning, providing an important open foundation for later agentic coding models.
- [Prompts Are Programs Too! Understanding How Developers Build Software Containing Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12447): Characterizes prompt programming through grounded-theory interviews with developers, arguing that some prompts function as programs in prompt-powered software systems.
- [Retrieval-Augmented Test Generation: How Far Are We?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12682): Evaluates retrieval-augmented unit-test generation, asking when retrieved context improves LLM-generated tests and when it adds noise.
- [CraftRTL: High-quality Synthetic Data Generation for Verilog Code Models with Correct-by-Construction Non-Textual Representations and Targeted Code Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12993): Generates high-quality synthetic Verilog data with correct-by-construction representations and targeted repair for code-model training.
- [Fast Decision Tree Learning Solves Hard Coding-Theoretic Problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13096): Studies decision-tree learning for coding-theory problems; here "coding" means information theory, not programming or software engineering.
- [RepoGenReflex: Enhancing Repository-Level Code Completion with Verbal Reinforcement and Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13122): RepoGenReflex combines repository-level retrieval with verbal reinforcement learning, using reflector feedback and an experience cache to improve code completion across large codebases.
- [Contextualized Data-Wrangling Code Generation in Computational Notebooks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13551): Builds contextualized data-wrangling code generation for notebooks by constructing datasets that expose textual, code, and data context across non-linear analysis workflows.
- [Eliciting Instruction-tuned Code Language Models' Capabilities to Utilize Auxiliary Function for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.13928): Tests query augmentation and response-prefix designs that let instruction-tuned code models reuse auxiliary functions during code generation.
- [PTD-SQL: Partitioning and Targeted Drilling with LLMs in Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.14082): PTD-SQL partitions text-to-SQL queries into problem types and drills LLMs on targeted reasoning patterns, matching or surpassing prior methods on Spider and BIRD.
- [Location is Key: Leveraging Large Language Model for Functional Bug Localization in Verilog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15186): Fine-tunes an open LLM for Verilog functional bug localization and reports higher pass@1 location accuracy than GPT-4 on an RTLLM-derived test set.
- [Boosting Code-Switching ASR with Mixture of Experts Enhanced Speech-Conditioned LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15905): Uses a speech-conditioned LLM, an MoE connector, interruption-token insertion and deletion, and two-stage training for code-switching speech recognition, so it is off-axis for coding models despite the "code-switching" wording.
- [DataGpt-SQL-7B: An Open-Source Language Model for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.15985): Builds compact open Text-to-SQL models from more than 20K SQL samples and preference data, with a code corrector to improve SQL validity.
- [MOSS: Enabling Code-Driven Evolution and Context Management for AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16120): Treats agent state as executable code plus context, maintaining multi-turn variable isolation and code-context consistency for adaptive LLM agents.
- [Selection of Prompt Engineering Techniques for Code Generation through Predicting Code Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.16416): Predicts query code complexity to choose among prompt-engineering techniques for code generation instead of applying a single prompting strategy to all tasks.
- [Programming Every Example: Lifting Pre-training Data Quality like Experts at Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17115): ProX uses small language models to generate per-example data-refinement programs, replacing fixed human heuristics for pretraining corpus quality control.
- [Graph Reasoning with Large Language Models via Pseudo-code Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.17906): Converts graph reasoning procedures into pseudo-code prompts so LLMs follow algorithmic steps for tasks such as connected components and shortest paths; this is adjacent reasoning work rather than a coding-model training method.
- [Compositional Hardness of Code in Large Language Models - A Probabilistic Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18028): Analyzes why long compositional code tasks become exponentially harder when solved in one context, motivating decomposed or multi-agent execution rather than a new code-model recipe.
- [Augmenting software engineering with AI and developing it further towards AI-assisted model-driven software engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18048): Position and agenda entry for AI-assisted model-driven software engineering; it frames MDE workflow integration rather than introducing model training.
- [Code Vulnerability Repair with Large Language Model Using Context-Aware Prompt Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18395): Designs context-aware prompts for buffer-overflow repair after finding Copilot detects many vulnerable snippets but repairs few of them.
- [Energy-Efficient Software Development: A Multi-dimensional Empirical Analysis of Stack Overflow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.19222): Mines 1,193 Stack Overflow questions to characterize developers' energy-consumption concerns, making it an empirical green-software study rather than a model-training contribution.
- [AMR-Evol: Adaptive Modular Response Evolution Elicits Better Knowledge Distillation for Large Language Models in Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.00558): Evolves teacher responses through modular refinement before distillation so open code models train on higher-quality synthetic code-generation data.
- [Sparse Autoencoders Reveal Temporal Difference Learning in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01280): Uses sparse autoencoders on Llama residual streams to identify temporal-difference-learning features during in-context reinforcement-learning tasks, making it mechanistic interpretability rather than SWE-specific modeling.
- [Codev-Bench: How Do LLMs Understand Developer-Centric Code Completion?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01353): Constructs developer-centric completion scenarios and evaluation signals for code-completion products, so the entry is mainly a benchmark rather than a new code model.
- [Enhancing LLM Fine-Tuning for Text-to-SQLs by SQL Quality Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01869): Uses SQL quality measurement against syntax criteria and database responses as a feedback loop for refining LLM-based Text-to-SQL fine-tuning outputs.
- [CHASE-SQL: Multi-Path Reasoning and Preference Optimized Candidate Selection in Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.01943): Combines divide-and-conquer SQL generation, execution-plan chain-of-thought, instance-aware synthetic demonstrations, and a fine-tuned pairwise selector for BIRD Text-to-SQL.
- [RLEF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02089): Grounds code LLMs in multi-step execution feedback with reinforcement learning, showing that models can learn to use compiler and test feedback rather than only resample independently.
- [Training Language Models on Synthetic Edit Sequences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02749): Uses synthetic edit trajectories to train code LMs to write and revise programs edit-by-edit, filling a data gap between single-pass generation and software-agent patching.
- [Demonstration Attack against In-Context Learning for Code Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02841): Defines DICE, a two-stage malicious-demonstration attack that selects and constructs bad in-context examples to mislead code-intelligence outputs without changing model weights.
- [Horizon-Length Prediction: Advancing Fill-in-the-Middle Capabilities for Code Generation with Lookahead Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03103): Adds a Horizon-Length Prediction objective so fill-in-the-middle code models learn remaining-span boundaries and improve file- and repository-level infilling without inference overhead.
- [Showing LLM-Generated Code Selectively Based on Confidence of LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03234): HonestCoder estimates code-generation confidence from multimodal similarity among generated programs and hides low-confidence outputs to reduce developer review burden.
- [Generating Equivalent Representations of Code By A Self-Reflection Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03351): Uses two LLMs in a self-reflection loop to generate semantics-preserving code representations such as comments and pseudocode under open or constrained settings.
- [Learning Code Preference via Synthetic Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.03837): Trains CodeFavor pairwise code-preference models from synthetic evolution data, including commits and critiques, to judge correctness, efficiency, security, and human taste.
- [DiffSpec: Differential Testing with LLMs using Natural Language Specifications and Code Artifacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.04249): Harness/testing entry; prompt-chains LLMs over natural-language specifications and code artifacts to generate differential tests that exposed bugs in eBPF runtimes and Wasm validators.
- [CodeDPO: Aligning Code Models with Self Generated and Verified Source Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.05605): Builds preference data from self-generated code and test cases, then applies DPO so code models prefer solutions that pass more tests and run more efficiently.
- [Large Language Models as Code Executors: An Exploratory Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06667): Tests whether LLMs can directly execute code snippets and return outputs across GPT, o1, DeepSeek, and Qwen-Coder families, positioning execution as a model capability probe.
- [Do Developers Adopt Green Architectural Tactics for ML-Enabled Systems? A Mining Software Repository Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.06708): Uses an LLM-based repository-mining mechanism to identify green architectural tactics in 168 open-source ML projects, making it empirical software-engineering analysis.
- [CursorCore: Assist Programming through Aligning Anything](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07002): Releases CursorCore models trained on 219K Programming-Instruct samples that combine coding history, current code, and user instructions for inline chat and automated code editing.
- [Do Current Language Models Support Code Intelligence for R Programming Language?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07793): Evaluates code-pretrained models on R-language code intelligence tasks, highlighting scientific-software gaps outside Java and Python.
- [Mitigating Gender Bias in Code Large Language Models via Model Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.07820): Builds CodeGenBias and FB-Score, then applies multi-granularity model editing to locate and adjust gender-biased behavior inside code LLMs.
- [What Makes Large Language Models Reason in (Multi-Turn) Code Generation?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08105): Systematically decomposes reasoning prompts, instruction prompts, re-prompting, and execution feedback to study why multi-turn code-generation gains appear on CodeContests and TACO.
- [Don't Transform the Code, Code the Transforms: Towards Precise Code Rewriting using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08806): Has LLMs synthesize explicit code-transformation programs from input-output examples, making rewrites inspectable and executable rather than directly editing target code.
- [Decoding Secret Memorization in Code LLMs Through Token-Level Characterization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08858): Proposes DESEC, a token-probability scoring and decoding method that distinguishes real from hallucinated secrets emitted by code LLMs to measure privacy leakage.
- [Test-driven Software Experimentation with LASSO: an LLM Benchmarking Example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.08911): Presents LASSO as an executable scripting platform for test-driven software experiments and uses LLM prompt benchmarking as its worked example.
- [One Step at a Time: Combining LLMs and Static Analysis to Generate Next-Step Hints for Programming Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09268): Combines LLM hint generation with static analysis so programming learners receive localized next-step guidance rather than full solutions.
- [Can Large Language Models Generate Geospatial Code?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.09738): Builds GeoCode-Eval and GeoCode-Bench with 5,000 geospatial tasks across cognition, interpretation, and innovation levels for testing geospatial code generation.
- [EffiCoder: Enhancing Code Generation in Large Language Models through Efficiency-Aware Fine-tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10209): Constructs Effi-Instruct by executing diverse candidate solutions and selecting the fastest, lowest-memory correct programs for efficiency-aware code-model fine-tuning.
- [Generalized Adversarial Code-Suggestions: Exploiting Contexts of LLM-based Code-Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10526): Formalizes adversarial code-suggestion attacks as trigger-pattern and embedding-map mechanisms, then tests directional-map and prompt-indexing attacks against code-completion defenses.
- [Learning from Imperfect Data: Towards Efficient Knowledge Distillation of Autoregressive Language Models for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.11371): Studies how imperfect teacher SQL outputs affect autoregressive distillation, targeting smaller Text-to-SQL models that can replace expensive LLMs.
- [Not All Votes Count! Programs as Verifiers Improve Self-Consistency of Language Models for Math Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12608): Translates natural-language math solutions into programs and filters inconsistent reasoning paths before answer aggregation, an adjacent verifier idea rather than a software-development model.
- [MSc-SQL: Multi-Sample Critiquing Small Language Models For Text-To-SQL Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.12916): Trains a small open Text-to-SQL model to critique multiple sampled SQL candidates jointly with metadata, improving open-model accuracy without relying on GPT-4-scale inference.
- [Deep learning-based software engineering: progress, challenges, and opportunities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13110): Surveys deep-learning applications across code generation, refactoring, and fault localization, emphasizing how models are trained or fine-tuned for SE tasks.
- [aiXcoder-7B: A Lightweight and Effective Large Language Model for Code Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13187): Trains a 7B code-completion model with multi-objective training, Structured Fill-in-the-Middle, and data sampling that captures inter-file relationships.
- [GeoCoder: Solving Geometry Problems by Generating Modular Code through Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13510): Fine-tunes VLMs to generate and execute modular code for geometry problems, using code execution to supply operations missing from pretrained multimodal models.
- [LLM-based Unit Test Generation via Property Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13542): Adds property-based retrieval augmentation for unit tests by retrieving Given, When, and Then phase information tailored to the focal method.
- [LightTransfer: Your Long-Context LLM is Secretly a Hybrid Model with Effortless Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.13846): Converts selected transformer layers into streaming-attention hybrid layers to reduce long-context KV-cache cost, so its relevance is inference efficiency rather than coding-specific training.
- [Towards Automated Verification of LLM-Synthesized C Programs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14835): SynVer biases LLM-synthesized C programs toward specifications that the Verified Software Toolchain can prove, combining synthesis with automated verification.
- [MCCoder: Streamlining Motion Control with LLM-Assisted Code Generation and Rigorous Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15154): Generates motion-control code through multitask decomposition, hybrid retrieval augmentation, iterative self-correction, and verification against a soft-motion controller.
- [Attention Is All You Need for LLM-based Code Vulnerability Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15288): LOVA uses line-by-line checking and LLM self-attention signals to localize vulnerable code spans more accurately over long code contexts.
- [Automated Proof Generation for Rust Code via Self-Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15756): SAFE bootstraps Rust proof generation by synthesizing proofs, verifying them symbolically, fine-tuning on correct proofs, and using incorrect proofs for self-debugging.
- [Self-Explained Keywords Empower Large Language Models for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.15966): SEK has the LLM extract, rank, and explain low-frequency problem keywords before code generation, improving HumanEval, MBPP, and APPS results across five models.
- [ChangeGuard: Validating Code Changes via Pairwise Learning-Guided Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16092): Uses pairwise learning-guided execution to compare original and modified functions, detecting semantics-changing refactorings that project regression tests often miss.
- [Building a Coding Assistant via the Retrieval-Augmented Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16229): CONAN pretrains a code-structure-aware retriever with code-document alignment and masked entity prediction, then feeds dual-view retrieved code into generation.
- [Safe Load Balancing in Software-Defined-Networking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16846): Combines deep reinforcement learning with control-barrier functions so SDN load-balancing policies satisfy safety constraints during training and testing.
- [GeoCode-GPT: A Large Language Model for Geospatial Code Generation Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17031): Builds a large language model for geospatial code generation tasks.
- [Process Supervision-Guided Policy Optimization for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17621): Trains line-level process reward models and uses them in policy optimization so code RL receives denser feedback than final unit-test outcomes.
- [In-Context Code-Text Learning for Bimodal Software Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18107): InCTRL converts 23 code-text SE tasks into in-context learning format with configurable prompt templates over pretrained CodeLLaMA models.
- [Context-Augmented Code Generation Using Programming Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18251): Represents code in a programming knowledge graph, prunes irrelevant context, and reranks retrieved snippets before feeding them to code LLMs.
- [WAFFLE: Finetuning Multi-Modal Models for Automated Front-End Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18362): Fine-tunes multimodal models for UI-to-HTML with structure-aware attention over HTML hierarchy and contrastive alignment between UI images and HTML code.
- [Aligning CodeLLMs with Direct Preference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18585): Applies DPO to CodeLLM alignment using preference pairs, arguing it supplies finer-grained ranking signals than coarse PPO reward rules.
- [Whose fault is it anyway? SILC: Safe Integration of LLM-Generated Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18703): SILC extends incorrectness separation logic to assign blame and sanitization obligations when AI-generated components violate safety assumptions after integration.
- [Bridge-Coder: Unlocking LLMs' Potential to Overcome Language Gaps in Low-Resource Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.18957): Addresses low-resource programming languages by bridging natural-language and code gaps with generated training data designed for languages such as Racket and D.
- [VisionCoder: Empowering Multi-Agent Auto-Programming for Image Processing with Hybrid LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19245): The linked arXiv record describes MaCTG, which coordinates LLM programming agents through a dynamic thought graph for role assignment, task planning, and hallucination control.
- [Combining LLM Code Generation with Formal Specifications and Reactive Program Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.19736): Splits code generation between an LLM and formal reactive synthesis so unusual high-risk logic is handled by specification-guided program synthesis.
- [CodeRosetta: Pushing the Boundaries of Unsupervised Code Translation for Parallel Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.20527): Trains an encoder-decoder translation model with HPC-specific pretraining objectives for C++ to CUDA and Fortran to C++ parallel-code translation.
- [SPICEPilot: Navigating SPICE Code Generation and Simulation with AI Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.20553): Generates a PySpice-based SPICE-code dataset and simulation framework with standardized metrics for circuit-level LLM code generation.
- [Geo-FuB: A method for constructing an Operator-Function knowledge base for geospatial code generation with large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.20975): Constructs an operator-function knowledge base for geospatial code generation with LLMs.
- [Are Decoder-Only Large Language Models the Silver Bullet for Code Search?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22240): Compares eleven decoder-only LLMs for code search in zero-shot and fine-tuned settings, showing fine-tuned CodeGemma can outperform encoder baselines on CoSQA+.
- [Rethinking Code Refinement: Learning to Judge Code Efficiency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.22375): Trains a code language model to judge which of two human or machine code versions is more efficient, avoiding repeated execution for every refinement comparison.
- [Multi-Programming Language Sandbox for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23074): MPLSandbox is a harness-shaped support entry: it detects the programming language, compiles and executes code in isolated sub-sandboxes, and returns compiler, execution, and code-analysis feedback for LLM training or deployment.
- [FVEval: Understanding Language Model Capabilities in Formal Verification of Digital Hardware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23299): Defines a formal-verification benchmark for LLMs across SystemVerilog assertion generation, debugging, and related hardware FV subtasks.
- [VISUALCODER: Guiding Large Language Models in Code Execution with Fine-grained Multimodal Chain-of-Thought Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23402): Aligns code snippets with visual control-flow graphs and multimodal chain-of-thought references to improve execution reasoning, error detection, and output prediction.
- [Generating Accurate OpenAPI Descriptions from Java Source Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23873): Statically extracts REST endpoint paths, HTTP methods, response codes, and request or response data models from Java Spring Boot source, outperforming Respector, Prophet, and springdoc-openapi on seven projects.
- [Envisioning responsible quantum software engineering and quantum artificial intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.23972): Frames responsible quantum software engineering and quantum AI research directions rather than introducing a code-model training recipe.
- [AlphaTrans: A Neuro-Symbolic Compositional Approach for Repository-Level Code Translation and Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.24117): Combines neural and symbolic components for repository-level code translation and validation.
- [SelfCodeAlign: Self-Alignment for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.24198): Self-aligns code LMs by extracting coding concepts, generating tasks, sampling responses with tests, sandbox-validating passing solutions, and instruction-tuning CodeQwen and StarCoder2 variants.
- [Personality-Guided Code Generation Using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00006): Prompts seven LLMs with task-aligned personality traits for code generation, improving pass rates across 23 of 28 model-dataset combinations.
- [RSL-SQL: Robust Schema Linking in Text-to-SQL Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00073): Combines bidirectional schema linking, contextual augmentation, binary full-or-simplified schema selection, and multi-turn self-correction for robust Text-to-SQL on BIRD and Spider.
- [Lingma SWE-GPT: An Open Development-Process-Centric Language Model for Automated Software Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00622): Trains 7B and 72B SWE-GPT models on development-process data, including developer reasoning, tool use, personnel interactions, and software evolution traces.
- [The Graph's Apprentice: Teaching an LLM Low Level Knowledge for Circuit Quality Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00843): Augments an HDL-oriented LLM with predictor networks regularized by GNN embeddings from LUT graphs to estimate circuit quality from code.
- [DemoCraft: Using In-Context Learning to Improve Code Generation in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.00865): Uses trainable latent concept tokens for demonstration selection in in-context code generation, roughly doubling pass@k on MBPP and HumanEval in the reported experiments.
- [PairSmell: A Novel Perspective Inspecting Software Modular Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01012): Models modularity smells as problematic class or file pairs, combining structural dependency evidence with learned pairwise inspection to detect architecture erosion.
- [INTERTRANS: Leveraging Transitive Intermediate Translations to Enhance LLM-Based Code Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01063): Uses transitive intermediate translations to improve LLM-based code translation.
- [A Deep Dive Into Large Language Model Code Generation Mistakes: What and Why?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01414): Manually analyzes non-syntactic mistakes in standalone and context-dependent LLM code generation, identifies seven mistake categories and six causes, and tests GPT-4 with ReAct for mistake-reason detection.
- [Generating executable oracles to check conformance of client code to requirements of JDK Javadocs using LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.01789): Uses LLMs to turn JDK Javadocs into executable test oracles for normal and exceptional Java library behavior, with most generated assertions compiling and matching intended properties.
- [Do advanced language models eliminate the need for prompt engineering in software engineering?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02093): Empirically compares prompt-engineering patterns across advanced LLMs on software-engineering tasks, testing whether stronger base models reduce task-specific prompt sensitivity.
- [Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning of Large Language Models for Unit Test Generation: An Empirical Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.02462): Compares full fine-tuning, LoRA, IA3, and prompt tuning for unit-test generation across thirteen LLMs, measuring syntax, pass@1, coverage, and mutation score.
- [Crystal: Illuminating LLM Abilities on Language and Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04156): Trains Crystal with a two-phase code and natural-language pretraining schedule whose code/language ratios are adjusted over time, then releases datasets, logs, 136 checkpoints, and ablations showing data-recipe effects.
- [Towards Optimizing SQL Generation via LLM Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04319): Introduces score-based and classification-based LLM routers for Text-to-SQL that learn to select the cheapest accurate model per query, trading BIRD accuracy against latency and cost.
- [CodeTree: Agent-guided Tree Search for Code Generation with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04329): Uses a unified tree to explore coding strategies, generate solutions, and refine them, with ranking, termination, and expansion guided by execution feedback and LLM-agent feedback.
- [An Empirical Study on the Potential of LLMs in Automated Software Refactoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04444): Evaluates LLMs on automated refactoring cases, separating behavior-preserving edits from harder transformations and analyzing where generated changes compile, pass tests, or alter semantics.
- [OpenCoder: The Open Cookbook for Top-Tier Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.04905): Releases a reproducible top-tier code LLM recipe, including weights, inference code, data cleaning and deduplication pipeline, synthetic annealing and SFT data, ablations, and training protocol.
- [Scattered Forest Search: Smarter Code Space Exploration with LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05010): Treats code generation as black-box search in code space; Scattered Forest Search diversifies candidates and uses feedback to escape local optima across HumanEval, MBPP, APPS, CodeContests, and LeetCode.
- [CodeLutra: Boosting LLM Code Generation via Preference-Guided Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05199): Fine-tunes smaller code LMs with iterative preference refinement over successful and failed code attempts, using mistakes as contrastive signal for data-science coding accuracy.
- [GitChameleon: Unmasking the Version-Switching Capabilities of Code Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.05830): Probes whether code-generation models can adapt API usage across library versions, exposing failures in version-specific reasoning that matter for real repository maintenance.
- [Anchor Attention, Small Cache: Code Generation With Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06680): Proposes AnchorCoder, a code-generation attention design with token-wise and layer-wise anchor attention that compresses contextual KV states and cuts cache needs by at least 70% while preserving most performance.
- [PDC & DM-SFT: A Road for LLM SQL Bug-Fix Enhancing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.06767): Improves SQL bug repair through Progressive Dataset Construction for breadth and depth data expansion plus Dynamic Mask SFT to reduce SQL-fix training disorientation.
- [ROCODE: Integrating Backtracking Mechanism and Program Analysis in Large Language Models for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07112): Integrates incremental program analysis with backtracking during autoregressive decoding so code LMs can roll back, constrain regeneration, and reduce compile or test failures before post-repair.
- [Software Performance Engineering for Foundation Model-Powered Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09580): Frames latency, cost, reliability, and quality bottlenecks that appear when foundation models are embedded in software systems, giving model-powered software a performance-engineering taxonomy.
- [Real-Time Adapting Routing (Rar): Improving Efficiency Through Continuous Learning in Software Powered by Layered Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09837): Learns a runtime router for layered foundation-model software, continuously updating model-selection policies so requests can shift between cheaper and stronger components.
- ["Should I Give Up Now?" Investigating LLM Pitfalls in Software Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.09916): Studies where LLMs abandon or mishandle software-engineering tasks, characterizing failure modes that affect code generation, repair, and developer-assistance workflows.
- [LLM-itation is the Sincerest Form of Data: Generating Synthetic Buggy Code Submissions for Computing Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10455): Generates privacy-preserving synthetic buggy programming submissions with GPT-4o and validates them by comparing test-failure distributions against real student code.
- [Large Language Models as Robust Data Generators in Software Analytics: Are We There Yet?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10565): Compares PTMs fine-tuned on human-written versus LLM-generated software-analytics data under nine adversarial attacks across clone detection, code summarization, and review-sentiment tasks.
- [An exploration of prompting LLMs to generate energy-efficient code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10599): Studies prompting methods for generating energy-efficient code with LLMs.
- [FlexFL: Flexible and Effective Fault Localization With Open-Source Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.10714): Uses open-source LLMs for fault localization, ranking suspicious code with flexible prompt and context settings rather than requiring proprietary model access.
- [REACCEPT: Automated Co-evolution of Production and Test Code Based on Dynamic Validation and Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11033): Co-evolves production and test code by asking LLMs for paired edits and using dynamic validation to accept only changes that preserve or improve executable behavior.
- [SRA-MCTS: Self-driven Reasoning Augmentation with Monte Carlo Tree Search for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11053): Synthesizes intermediate reasoning paths with Monte Carlo Tree Search, translates them into executable code, and uses self-generated trajectories to improve smaller code models without extra supervision.
- [Deep Learning-based Code Reviews: A Paradigm Shift or a Double-Edged Sword?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11401): Reviews deep-learning code-review systems and their risks, covering automated comment generation, defect detection, reviewer support, and the possibility of misleading review feedback.
- [CodeXEmbed: A Generalist Embedding Model Family for Multiligual and Multi-task Code Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.12644): Builds a generalist embedding model family for multilingual and multitask code retrieval.
- [Towards Specification-Driven LLM-Based Generation of Embedded Automotive Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13269): Studies spec2code, combining LLM code generation with formal ACSL or natural-language specifications, critics, backprompting, and fine-tuning for embedded automotive software.
- [DSTC: Direct Preference Learning with Only Self-Generated Tests and Code to Improve Code LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.13611): Builds preference pairs only from self-generated tests and code using minimax selection and test-code concatenation, then applies DPO or KTO to improve pass@1 without external annotations.
- [BugSpotter: Automated Generation of Code Debugging Exercises](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.14303): Generates buggy code from programming problem descriptions and verifies synthesized bugs with test suites, producing debugging exercises comparable to instructor-authored versions.
- [AssertLLM: Generating Hardware Verification Assertions from Design Specifications via Multi-LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.14436): Coordinates multiple LLMs to translate hardware design specifications into verification assertions, adding cross-model checking to HDL assertion generation.
- [Don't Mesh with Me: Generating Constructive Solid Geometry Instead of Meshes by Fine-Tuning a Code-Generation LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.15279): Fine-tunes a code-generation LLM on CSG Python scripts converted from BREP mechanical parts and GPT-4 natural-language annotations, enabling natural-language and positional completion of precise CAD geometry.
- [Fault Localization from the Semantic Code Search Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.17230): Recasts fault localization as semantic code search, using learned code-query matching to retrieve suspicious statements or files for a failure report.
- [TransferFuzz: Fuzzing with Historical Trace for Verifying Propagated Vulnerability Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.18347): Uses historical execution traces to guide fuzzing of vulnerability propagation, checking whether copied or migrated vulnerable code remains exploitable.
- [VeCoGen: Automating Generation of Formally Verified C Code With Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.19275): Combines LLM candidate generation with ANSI/ISO C formal specifications, natural-language specs, tests, and iterative verifier-guided improvement to produce verified C programs.
- [o1-Coder: an o1 Replication for Coding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00154): Replicates o1-style coding by training a test-case generator, using MCTS to create reasoning-rich code data, and iteratively fine-tuning policy models from pseudocode to full code.
- [Generating a Low-code Complete Workflow via Task Decomposition and RAG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00239): Decomposes low-code workflow requests into subtasks and uses retrieval-augmented generation to assemble executable workflow components rather than a single monolithic code output.
- [Language Models in Software Development Tasks: An Experimental Analysis of Energy and Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00329): Measures 18 LLM families on software-development tasks across commodity and AI GPUs, comparing full-precision and quantized local deployment to expose energy-accuracy trade-offs for coding models.
- [CoRNStack: High-Quality Contrastive Data for Better Code Retrieval and Reranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01007): Curates contrastive code-retrieval data with consistency filtering and mined hard negatives, then trains retrievers and rerankers for bug localization and repository search.
- [When Fine-Tuning LLMs Meets Data Privacy: An Empirical Study of Federated Learning in LLM-Based Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01072): Applies federated fine-tuning to LLM-based program repair so private code repositories can improve APR models without centralizing proprietary code.
- [AutoPLC: Generating Vendor-Aware Structured Text for Programmable Logic Controllers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02410): Generates vendor-aware PLC Structured Text from natural-language requirements using vendor API libraries, annotated cases, LLM reranking, API recommendation, and IDE-based validation.
- [Exploring the Potential of Llama Models in Automated Code Refinement: A Replication Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02789): Replicates automated code refinement with CodeLlama and Llama 2, showing tuned open models can approach ChatGPT on review-driven edits while refactoring-style changes remain easier than new-code additions.
- [Does Few-Shot Learning Help LLM Performance in Code Synthesis?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.02906): Measures how few-shot examples change code-synthesis accuracy, separating gains from example selection, prompt length, and problem difficulty.
- [PerfCodeGen: Improving Performance of LLM Generated Code with Execution Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03578): Improves generated-code runtime without training by feeding execution-time measurements from test cases into self-refinement loops across open and closed LLMs.
- [Synergizing LLMs and Knowledge Graphs: A Novel Approach to Software Repository-Related Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03815): Builds repository question answering around a software knowledge graph, letting the LLM ground answers in code entities, relationships, and project artifacts.
- [Integrating Various Software Artifacts for Better LLM-based Bug Localization and Program Repair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.03905): Feeds LLM bug localization and repair with multiple artifacts such as reports, tests, traces, and code context so repairs are constrained by more than the buggy file.
- [HiVeGen – Hierarchical LLM-based Verilog Generation for Scalable Chip Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05393): Generates scalable Verilog hierarchically, decomposing chip-design specifications into modules before producing and validating RTL code.
- [GEE-OPs: an operator knowledge base for geospatial code generation on the Google Earth Engine platform powered by large language models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05587): Builds an operator knowledge base for LLM-powered Google Earth Engine code generation.
- [AlphaVerus: Bootstrapping Formally Verified Code Generation through Self-Improving Translation and Treefinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.06176): Bootstraps verified-code generation by translating programs from higher-resource languages, refining candidates with Treefinement, and filtering verifier reward hacking.
- [The Rise and Down of Babel Tower: Investigating the Evolution Process of Multilingual Code Large Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.07298): Tracks multilingual capability formation during code-LLM pretraining via working-language and language-transfer neurons, then uses the Babel Tower hypothesis to redesign multilingual code pretraining corpora.
- [Scalable, Validated Code Translation of Entire Projects using Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08035): Partitions whole-project code translation into independently translated fragments, then uses I/O equivalence, feature mapping, and type-compatibility checks to guide Go-to-Rust repair and validation.
- [Quantifying the benefits of code hints for refactoring deprecated Java APIs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08041): Compares symbolic synthesis and LLM refactoring engines for deprecated JDK APIs, showing Javadoc code hints sharply improve correct automated migrations.
- [Doc2OracLL: Investigating the Impact of Documentation on LLM-Based Test Oracle Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.09360): Examines how Javadoc comments and related context affect LLM-based test-oracle generation, isolating documentation components that help produce stronger Java assertions.
- [ROUTE: Robust Multitask Tuning and Collaboration for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10138): Fine-tunes open Text-to-SQL models on synthetic SQL tasks including schema linking, noise correction, and continuation writing, then coordinates the tasks with collaborative prompting.
- [Enhancing Automated Loop Invariant Generation for Complex Programs with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10483): Uses static analysis to extract loop facts, prompts LLMs for candidate invariants, and applies an LLM evaluator to strengthen, weaken, or reject invariants for complex programs.
- [Optimizing AI-Assisted Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.10953): Studies optimization levers for AI-assisted coding, including prompt choices, model selection, and feedback signals that improve generated-code quality.
- [PERC: Plan-As-Query Example Retrieval for Underrepresented Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12447): Retrieves few-shot code examples by converting source code into pseudocode plans and querying for algorithmic plans, improving underrepresented-language generation across CodeContests, HumanEval, and MultiPL-E.
- [Solid-SQL: Enhanced Schema-linking based In-context Learning for Robust Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.12522): Trains a robust schema-linking model with LLM data augmentation and uses two-round structural-similarity example retrieval to harden Text-to-SQL against adversarial schema perturbations.
- [GenX: Mastering Code and Test Generation with Execution Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13464): Co-trains code and test generators with execution feedback, augmenting code-test data and ranking generated tests and solutions for iterative APPS improvements.
- [Transducer Tuning: Efficient Model Adaptation for Software Tasks Using Code Property Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.13467): Adapts large code models by fusing Code Property Graph vectors into code embeddings through a trainable transducer, reducing trainable parameters for summarization, assertion generation, and translation.
- [CAD-Recode: Reverse Engineering CAD Code From Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14042): Represents CAD sketch-extrude histories as executable Python code, combines a point-cloud projector with a small LLM decoder, and trains on one million procedural CAD sequences for point-cloud-to-code reconstruction.
- [Is This You, LLM? Recognizing AI-written Programs with Multilingual Code Stylometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14611): Trains a transformer encoder classifier and releases H-AIRosettaMP with 121,247 snippets across ten languages to detect AI-written programs from multilingual code stylometry.
- [Helping LLMs Improve Code Generation Using Feedback from Testing and Static Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.14841): Uses test failures and static-analysis vulnerability reports as feedback for open LLMs to judge and repair generated C code.
- [Scylla: Translating an Applicative Subset of C to Safe Rust](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15042): Defines an applicative subset of C and translates it into safe Rust, emphasizing language restrictions and correctness-preserving translation rather than unconstrained C-to-Rust generation.
- [Reasoning Through Execution: Unifying Process and Outcome Rewards for Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15118): Uses tree-structured search, execution metrics, and self-critique to unify process and outcome rewards for code generation, improving correctness and efficiency.
- [HPC-Coder-V2: Studying Code LLMs Across Low-Resource Parallel Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15178): Fine-tunes and studies a specialized HPC code LLM across low-resource parallel languages, identifying obstacles to parallel code generation and reporting the best open-source performance in that setting.
- [Improving FIM Code Completions via Context & Curriculum Based Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.16589): Builds curriculum and context examples from hard repository completion patterns using semantic and static-analysis tools, then fine-tunes StarCoder and DeepSeek FIM models under latency constraints.
- [A Plug-and-Play Natural Language Rewriter for Natural Language to SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17068): Wraps NL2SQL systems with Checker, Reflector, and Rewriter modules that use database contents and generated-SQL correctness to rewrite flawed natural-language queries.
- [WarriorCoder: Learning from Expert Battles to Augment Code Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17395): Creates training data by staging battles among expert code LMs judged by impartial evaluators, then fine-tunes same-size models without relying on proprietary LLM data.
- [Condor: A Code Discriminator Integrating General Semantics With Code Details](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17429): Trains the Condor code discriminator with contrastive representation learning and code-modification intermediate data so it can rerank generated programs by subtle code-detail differences without execution.
- [Investigating Large Language Models for Code Vulnerability Detection: An Experimental Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.18260): Fine-tunes four open LLMs for code vulnerability detection and compares them with graph and sequence baselines across five CVD datasets, including class imbalance and length analyses.
- [Repository Structure-Aware Training Makes SLMs Better Issue Resolver](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.19031): Builds ReSAT training data from open-source issues and pull requests, using progressive localization data and context-based code-edit data to improve SLM issue resolving and repository understanding.
- [Assessing the Robustness of LLM-based NLP Software via Automated Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.21016): Uses automated testing to perturb inputs for LLM-based NLP software, measuring whether model-backed components remain robust under generated test cases.
- [SWE-Gym](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.21139): Provides executable training environments for real-world software-engineering agents and trains both agents and verifiers on SWE-style tasks.
- [Few-shot code translation via task-adapted prompt learning](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2024.112002): Improves few-shot code translation with task-adapted prompt learning.
- [Generating P4 Dataplanes Using LLMs](https://doi.org/10.1109/hpsr62440.2024.10635926): Tests LLM generation of P4 dataplane programs, targeting network-program synthesis rather than general-purpose Python or Java code.
- [Domain-Adapted LLMs for VLSI Design and Verification: A Case Study on Formal Verification](https://doi.org/10.1109/vts60656.2024.10538589): Adapts LLMs to VLSI formal-verification tasks, focusing on domain data and verification prompts for hardware-design code.
- [GlueTest: Testing Code Translation via Language Interoperability](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme58944.2024.00061): Tests code translation by exploiting language interoperability, running translated and source components together to reveal semantic mismatches.
- [Enhancing Code Generation Through Retrieval of Cross-Lingual Semantic Graphs](https://doi.org/10.1109/apsec65559.2024.00026): Retrieves cross-lingual semantic graphs as generation context so code models can transfer implementation patterns across programming languages.
- [StepCoder: Improving Code Generation with Reinforcement Learning from Compiler Feedback](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-long.251): Trains code generation with reinforcement learning from compiler feedback, using compile errors as step-level signals for syntactically valid and executable programs.
- [Leveraging LLMs for Program Verification](https://doi.org/10.34727/2024/isbn.978-3-85448-065-5_16): Applies LLMs to program verification tasks such as invariant or proof generation, using verifier feedback to check candidate reasoning about code.
- [Can a Llama Be a Watchdog? Exploring Llama 3 and Code Llama for Static Application Security Testing](https://doi.org/10.1109/csr61664.2024.10679444): Evaluates Llama 3 and Code Llama as static-application-security-testing classifiers, comparing open LLM vulnerability findings with SAST-style expectations.
- [How Prompt Specificity Affects Edge Case Handling in LLM-Generated Code: An Empirical Evaluation](https://doi.org/10.69987/aimlr.2024.50411): Measures how explicit edge-case prompts affect generated-code behavior, testing whether specificity improves coverage of boundary conditions.
- [Exploring ChatGPT's code refactoring capabilities: An empirical study](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.eswa.2024.123602): Empirically evaluates ChatGPT on code refactoring tasks, checking whether generated transformations preserve behavior while improving structure.
- [NL2SQL is a solved problem... Not!](https://www.vldb.org/cidrdb/2024/nl2sql-is-a-solved-problem-not.html): Argues that Text-to-SQL remains brittle in realistic settings, highlighting schema ambiguity, enterprise constraints, and execution-grounded errors that model papers often hide.
- [Code Gradients: Towards Automated Traceability of LLM-Generated Code](https://doi.org/10.1109/re59067.2024.00038): Traces LLM-generated code back to requirements and prompts, giving generated artifacts provenance links for review and compliance.
- [Enhancing Text-to-SQL Parsing through Question Rewriting and Execution-Guided Refinement](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.120): Rewrites ambiguous natural-language questions and refines SQL with execution feedback, improving Text-to-SQL parsing through a correction loop.
- [ProConSuL: Project Context for Code Summarization with LLMs](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-industry.65): Adds project-level context to code summarization prompts so LLMs summarize code with repository names, dependencies, and surrounding artifacts in view.
- [On the Effectiveness of Large Language Models in Statement-level Code Summarization](https://doi.org/10.1109/qrs62785.2024.00030): Evaluates LLMs on statement-level code summarization, exposing how granularity and local context affect generated explanations.
- [RRGcode: Deep hierarchical search-based code generation](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jss.2024.111982): Uses hierarchical search for code generation, decomposing the solution space so generated programs can be explored and refined at multiple levels.
- [LLM-Based Class Diagram Derivation from User Stories with Chain-of-Thought Promptings](https://doi.org/10.1109/compsac61105.2024.00017): Derives UML class diagrams from user stories with chain-of-thought prompting, turning requirements text into structured design artifacts.
- [NaturalCodeBench: Examining Coding Performance Mismatch on HumanEval and Natural User Queries](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-acl.471): Shows that HumanEval-style scores can overstate coding usefulness by comparing model performance on natural user coding queries.
- [Can LLMs Generate Green Code - A Comprehensive Study Through LeetCode](https://doi.org/10.1109/igsc64514.2024.00017): Studies whether LLM-generated LeetCode solutions are energy efficient, comparing correctness with runtime and energy-oriented code quality.
- [Cross-Modal Retrieval-enhanced code Summarization based on joint learning for retrieval and generation](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.infsof.2024.107527): Jointly trains retrieval and generation for code summarization, using cross-modal context to improve summaries beyond local code tokens.
- [DeepMig: A transformer-based approach to support coupled library and code migrations](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.infsof.2024.107588): Uses a transformer model to support coupled library and source-code migrations, predicting coordinated API and code changes.
- [Can Large Language Model Detect Plagiarism in Source Code?](https://doi.org/10.1109/fllm63129.2024.10852497): Evaluates LLMs for source-code plagiarism detection, testing whether model judgments capture semantic similarity beyond superficial token overlap.
- [LLM vs HLS for RTL Code Generation: Friend or Foe?](https://doi.org/10.1109/ats64447.2024.10915452): Compares LLM-based RTL generation with high-level synthesis, clarifying where language models complement or fall short of traditional hardware-generation flows.
- [Free your mouse! Command Large Language Models to Generate Code to Format Word Documents](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.902): Uses LLM-generated scripts to automate Word document formatting, treating document editing as code generation over office APIs.
- [A Study on C Code Defect Detection with Fine-Tuned Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/apsec65559.2024.00055): Fine-tunes LLMs for C defect detection and compares their vulnerability or bug classification behavior with task-specific baselines.
- [A^3-CodGen: A Repository-Level Code Generation Framework for Code Reuse With Local-Aware, Global-Aware, and Third-Party-Library-Aware](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2024.3486195): Generates repository-level code with local, global, and third-party-library awareness so candidates can reuse in-project APIs and external dependencies.
- [One-to-many testing for code generation from (just) natural language](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.findings-emnlp.902): Generates multiple tests from a natural-language coding prompt to validate one-to-many possible implementations rather than a single reference program.
- [Take Loads Off Your Developers: Automated User Story Generation using Large Language Model](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme58944.2024.00082): Uses LLMs to generate user stories from project context, supporting requirements engineering before code generation.
- [How Do Your Code LLMs perform? Empowering Code Instruction Tuning with Really Good Data](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.emnlp-main.777): Builds higher-quality code instruction-tuning data and analyzes how data filtering changes downstream code-LLM performance.
- [Automated Test Cases Generator for IEC 61131-3 Structured Text Based Dynamic Symbolic Execution](https://doi.org/10.1109/tc.2024.3351285): Generates test cases for IEC 61131-3 Structured Text by combining dynamic symbolic execution with automation for PLC software.
- [Decoupling SQL query hardness parsing for text-to-SQL](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neucom.2024.129293): Separates SQL hardness parsing from generation, letting Text-to-SQL systems condition on predicted query difficulty before producing SQL.
- [Predicting ChatGPT’s Ability to Solve Complex Programming Challenges](https://doi.org/10.1109/bigdata62323.2024.10825139): Predicts when ChatGPT will solve complex programming challenges, modeling task features that correlate with success or failure.
- [AICodeReview: Advancing code quality with AI-enhanced reviews](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.softx.2024.101677): Implements AI-assisted code review to surface quality issues and suggested improvements during review workflows.
- [Multi-stage guided code generation for Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.engappai.2024.109491): Guides LLM code generation through multiple stages, decomposing problem understanding, candidate generation, and refinement instead of relying on one-shot output.
- [Towards LLM-Assisted System Testing for Microservices](https://doi.org/10.1109/icdcsw63686.2024.00011): Applies LLMs to microservice system testing, generating or prioritizing tests across service interactions rather than isolated functions.
- [Automated Refactoring of Non-Idiomatic Python Code With Pythonic Idioms](https://doi.org/10.1109/tse.2024.3420886): Automatically refactors non-idiomatic Python into Pythonic forms while checking that transformed code preserves behavior.
- [On Improving Repository-Level Code QA for Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.18653/v1/2024.acl-srw.28): Improves repository-level code question answering by retrieving and packaging relevant project context for the LLM.
- [Advanced Smart Contract Vulnerability Detection Using Large Language Models](https://doi.org/10.1109/csnet64211.2024.10851734): Applies large language models to smart-contract vulnerability detection, a code-security analysis task rather than general code generation.
- [Large Language Models, Fine Tuning, Code Generation](https://doi.org/10.1109/cando-epe65072.2024.10772760): Examines fine-tuning large language models for code generation, based on publisher metadata rather than a fully available abstract.
- [Using Large Language Model to Label Data in Determining Software Quality Classes: ChatGPT](https://doi.org/10.1109/asyu62119.2024.10757027): Uses ChatGPT as a data-labeling aid for software quality classes, making it a dataset annotation and quality-classification support study.
- [Private-Keep Out? Understanding How Developers Account for Code Visibility in Unit Testing](https://doi.org/10.1109/icsme58944.2024.00037): Studies how developers decide whether private code should be exposed or hidden in unit tests, so it is testing-practice evidence rather than model training.
- [LLM for Complex Signal Processing in FPGA-based Software Defined Radios: A Case Study on FFT](https://doi.org/10.1109/vtc2024-fall63153.2024.10757597): Uses an LLM in an FPGA software-defined-radio FFT case study, connecting code generation to domain-specific signal-processing implementation.
- [UniBin: Assembly semantic-enhanced binary vulnerability detection without disassembly](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ins.2024.121605): Detects binary vulnerabilities by learning assembly semantics directly, avoiding a separate disassembly step in the vulnerability-analysis pipeline.
- [Simulink Design of Optimized Turbo Code for Next-Generation Wireless Networks: A 5G and Beyond Perspective](https://doi.org/10.1109/icdcece60827.2024.10548833): Designs optimized turbo-code systems in Simulink for wireless networks, which is adjacent signal-processing software engineering rather than a code LLM method.
- [Code Confabulator: Harnessing LLMs to Compile Code for Visualization](https://doi.org/10.1109/icccnt61001.2024.10724543): Uses LLMs to compile or generate code for visualization tasks, positioning visualization scripting as a software-generation target.
- [Co-Synthesis of Code and Formal Models Using Large Language Models and Functors](https://doi.org/10.1109/milcom61039.2024.10773930): Co-generates executable code and formal models with LLMs and functor-style structure, linking code synthesis to checkable modeling artifacts.
- [Cost-Sensitive Hypergraph Learning With Structure Quality Preservation for IoT Software Defect Prediction](https://doi.org/10.1109/ojcoms.2024.3514774): Uses cost-sensitive hypergraph learning to preserve structural quality while predicting defects in IoT software.
- [Learning To Code With Text-Bison-001:A Beginner-Friendly Explainer for Python, C, Java](https://doi.org/10.1109/icccnt61001.2024.10724566): Uses Text-Bison-001 as an explanatory assistant for beginner Python, C, and Java learning, making it education support rather than a new code model.
- [AIssistDM: A Plugin to Assist Non-specialist Decision-Makers in Search-Based Software Engineering Tools](https://doi.org/10.5753/sbes.2024.3567): Adds a ChatGPT-backed plugin that suggests domain parameters for search-based software-engineering tools, making it an Agent Harness or tooling candidate.
- [Improving Reconstruction Fidelity in Generative Face Video Coding using High-Frequency Shuttling](https://doi.org/10.1109/vcip63160.2024.10849880): Improves generative face video coding with high-frequency shuttling, which is off-axis for software-development code models.
- [EMGL: Enhancing Mandarin-English Code-Switching Speech Recognition using Generative Labeling Technique](https://doi.org/10.1109/icct62411.2024.10946392): Uses generative labeling for Mandarin-English code-switching speech recognition; the "code-switching" task is linguistic, not software code.
- [Fusion of Contrastive Learning and Heterogeneous Graph for Code Summarization](https://doi.org/10.1109/eit63098.2024.10762491): Combines contrastive learning with heterogeneous graph representations for code summarization, targeting semantic alignment between source structure and generated summaries.
- [Security Analysis of Large Language Models on API Misuse Programming Repair](https://doi.org/10.1155/2024/7135765): Builds APImisRepair for monitoring-resource-management API misuse and shows LLM repair failures can be partly mitigated with APImisAP's misuse-aware prompting.
- [SPDZCoder: Combining Expert Knowledge with LLMs for Generating Privacy-Computing Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00363): Translates Python into MP-SPDZ privacy-computing code through expert-derived transformation rules and a three-stage LLM-assisted pipeline, evaluated on the SPDZEval benchmark.
- [MCP-Solver: Integrating Language Models with Constraint Programming Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.00539): Bridges LLMs to MiniZinc, PySAT, and Z3 through the Model Context Protocol, using iterative validation and editing to keep constraint-solving artifacts consistent.
- [A case study on the transformative potential of AI in software engineering on LeetCode and ChatGPT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03639): Compares GPT-4o and LeetCode user Python solutions on quality, understandability, runtime, memory, and generalization to unseen problems.
- [How to Select Pre-Trained Code Models for Reuse? A Learning Perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.03783): Evaluates model-reuse selection across 100 public code models and trains proxy models plus latent-feature transferability measures to choose candidates without brute-force fine-tuning.
- [On the Impact of Requirements Smells in Prompts: The Case of Automated Traceability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.04810): Tests how ambiguous or inconsistent requirement smells in prompts affect LLM trace-link generation between requirements and code.
- [SWE-Fixer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.05040): Trains open retrieval and code-editing models on 110K GitHub issues and patches for efficient repository-level issue resolution.
- [Towards a Probabilistic Framework for Analyzing and Improving LLM-Enabled Software](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.06370): A model, training, architecture, or algorithm entry for software-development agents and code intelligence. Its value is to track capability-relevant model-side progress under the round15 Model-screening rule.
- [LlamaRestTest: Effective REST API Testing with Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08598): Fine-tunes small Llama models to generate REST API test inputs and infer inter-parameter dependencies from OpenAPI specifications.
- [Software testing for extended reality applications: a systematic mapping study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.08909): Maps 34 XR software-testing studies across test facets, techniques, evaluation methods, datasets, and tools, so it is a Survey candidate.
- [Green-Code: Learning to Optimize Energy Efficiency in Llm-Based Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11006): Trains a reinforcement-learning early-exit controller for LLM code generation, balancing accuracy, latency, and energy consumption during inference.
- [Directional Diffusion-Style Code Editing Pre-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.12079): Pretrains DivoT5 with directional diffusion-style code evolution tasks, denoising pre-edit snippets into post-edit snippets and transforming intermediate versions toward the final edit.
- [From Critique to Clarity: A Pathway to Faithful and Personalized Code Explanations with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14731): Combines prompt enhancement, self-correction, personalization, external tools, and multiple LLM agents to generate faithful code explanations, making it an Agent Harness candidate.
- [Faster Configuration Performance Bug Testing with Neural Dual-Level Prioritization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.15392): Prioritizes configuration performance bug testing with two neural language models, one estimating bug types as oracles and another ranking option and value-range search.
- [MultiMend: Multilingual Program Repair with Context Augmentation and Multi-Hunk Patch Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16044): Fine-tunes a code language model for multilingual APR, augmenting buggy-function context with retrieved lines and constructing multi-hunk patches to repair 2,227 bugs across six benchmarks.
- [Large Language Model Critics for Execution-Free Evaluation of Code Changes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16655): Builds reference-aware LLM critics for execution-free evaluation of repository-level code changes, giving coding agents intermediate semantic feedback when build or test signals are sparse.
- [Optimizing Code Runtime Performance Through Context-Aware Retrieval-Augmented Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.16692): Optimizes generated code by retrieving historical examples, adding control-flow graph context, and prompting LLMs through an analogy-driven in-context refinement pipeline.
- [ExeCoder: Empowering Large Language Models with Executability Representation for Code Translation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.18460): Injects executability representations into code LLM training for more reliable code translation.
- [CoDe: Blockwise Control for Denoising Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.00968): Introduces controlled denoising, a gradient-free inference-time guidance method for diffusion models, which is off-axis for software-development code models despite the acronym.
- [Analysis of Student-LLM Interaction in a Software Engineering Project](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01273): Analyzes 126 students' ChatGPT and Copilot interactions over a semester, comparing generated code quality, complexity, and human intervention in an education setting.
- [PRLCoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.01715): Builds process-supervised code RL by automatically labeling statement-level mutations and refactorings with compilation and execution checks.
- [AsserT5: Test Assertion Generation Using a Fine-Tuned Code Language Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02708): Fine-tunes a code language model for automated test assertion generation.
- [Harnessing Large Language Models for Curated Code Reviews](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03425): Builds a curation pipeline for higher-quality code-review comments and downstream code-refinement training data.
- [CTRL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03492): Trains critic models through reinforcement learning so iterative critique-revision and generative reward modeling improve code generation.
- [Resource-Efficient & Effective Code Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03617): Studies resource-efficient code summarization methods.
- [Should Code Models Learn Pedagogically? A Preliminary Evaluation of Curriculum Learning for Real-World Software Engineering Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.03806): Tests curriculum learning for CodeT5 on clone detection and code summarization, ordering training by code length and cyclomatic complexity and finding saturation, forgetting, and shortcut-learning effects.
- [EnseSmells : Deep ensemble and programming language models for automated code smells detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.05012): Builds a code-smell detector that fuses structural design features with statistical semantics from pretrained programming-language models, then analyzes embedding choices across four smell types.
- [Combining Large Language Models with Static Analyzers for Code Review Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06633): Combines rule-based static-analysis signals with LLM generation to improve code-review feedback beyond purely learned review models.
- [Harness Local Rewards for Global Benefits: Effective Text-to-Video Generation Alignment with Patch-level Reward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.06812): Aligns text-to-video diffusion models with patch-level reward models and Gran-DPO, which belongs with video-generation Model rather than software development.
- [Optimizing Datasets for Code Summarization: Is Code-Comment Coherence Enough?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07611): Studies code-comment coherence as a criterion for optimizing code-summarization datasets.
- [Counterexample Guided Program Repair Using Zero-Shot Learning and MaxSAT-based Fault Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.07786): Combines MaxSAT fault localization with zero-shot LLM synthesis in a CEGIS loop, feeding counterexamples back to repair minimal buggy program sketches.
- [Intention is All you Need: Refining your Code from your Intention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08172): Extracts reviewer intentions into template categories, then uses LLMs to generate code revisions conditioned on the intended refactoring or fix.
- [From PowerPoint UI Sketches to Web-Based Applications: Pattern-Driven Code Generation for GIS Dashboard Development Using Knowledge-Augmented LLMs, Context-Aware Visual Prompting, and the React Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08756): A model, training, or algorithmic contribution for software-engineering agents and code models. Core idea: focus on pattern-driven code generation for gis dashboard development using knowledge-augmented llms, context-aware visual prompting, and the react framework as a capability-relevant model reference.
- [Knowledge-Enhanced Program Repair for Data Science Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.09771): Repairs LLM-generated data-science code with knowledge-graph API retrieval, bug-knowledge enrichment, and AST-level localization.
- [VisPath: Automated Visualization Code Synthesis via Multi-Path Reasoning and Feedback-Driven Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11140): Generates visualization code through multi-path reasoning: expands underspecified prompts, executes candidate scripts, scores visual outputs, and aggregates feedback into a final chart.
- [SAFE-SQL: Self-Augmented In-Context Learning with Fine-grained Example Selection for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.11438): Generates multiple synthetic Text-to-SQL examples for each test query, filters them with three relevance checks, and uses the selected self-augmented examples for in-context SQL generation.
- [Circuit Representation Learning with Masked Gate Modeling and Verilog-AIG Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.12732): Pretrains circuit representations with masked gate modeling in latent space and Verilog-AIG alignment so GNNs learn circuit function from equivalent Verilog code.
